## 1. `michi-toon` (`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/crates/michi-toon/src/`)

**Purpose:** Renders a flat, tabular list of rows into the TOON text format — a header line (`type_name[count]{field,field,...}:`) stating field names once, followed by comma-separated rows. Also does the reverse for `serde`-serializable structs when the `serde` feature is on.

**Public API:**
- `pub struct Value` (enum): `Str(CompactString)`, `Int(i64)`, `Float(f64)`, `Bool(bool)`, `Null`. `From&lt;&amp;str|String|CompactString|Cow&lt;str&gt;|i32|u32|i64|u64|usize|f32|f64|bool|Option&lt;String&gt;&gt;` impls. `Display`.
- `pub struct ToonOptions { type_name, fields: Vec&lt;String&gt;, rows: Vec&lt;Vec&lt;Value&gt;&gt;, total_count: Option&lt;usize&gt;, hints: Vec&lt;String&gt;, max_cell_len: usize }` (`#[non_exhaustive]`), builder methods `new()`, `.total_count()`, `.hints()`, `.max_cell_len()` (default 200), and `.validate() -&gt; Result&lt;(), ToonError&gt;` — **opt-in**, not called automatically by `render_toon()`.
- `pub fn render_toon(opts: &amp;ToonOptions) -&gt; String` — never errors; sanitizes bad input in place (structural chars in headers/hints are replaced with `_`) rather than rejecting it.
- `pub enum ToonError { InvalidTypeName, InvalidFieldName, RowLengthMismatch, InvalidItem }`.
- `pub fn escape_value(v: &amp;str) -&gt; Cow&lt;str&gt;` / `escape_value_quoted(v: &amp;str) -&gt; String`.
- `#[cfg(feature = "serde")] pub fn list&lt;T: Serialize&gt;(type_name, items: &amp;[T]) -&gt; Result&lt;String, ToonError&gt;` — serializes each item via `serde_json::to_value`, requires the result be a JSON object (struct/map); scalars/arrays error with `InvalidItem`.

**Cargo features:** `default = []` (zero deps beyond `compact_str`, which is a mandatory dep, not feature-gated). `serde = ["dep:serde", "dep:serde_json"]` — required for `toon::list()`. `schemars` — derives `JsonSchema` on `ToonOptions`/`Value`.

**Usage patterns (from examples/tests):**
- Manual construction: build `ToonOptions` by hand with typed `Value` rows (the `examples/basic_toon.rs` / README pattern) — this is the primary intended path for a CLI tool with already-structured data.
- `toon::list()` for "I have a `Vec&lt;MySerdeStruct&gt;`, just render it" (needs `serde` feature). Field order comes from the first non-empty item's serialized key order (`serde_json::to_value` with the workspace's `preserve_order` feature enabled, so struct field order is preserved, not alphabetized).
- Long cell strings are auto-truncated per-cell via `michi_truncate::truncate_inline` when they exceed `max_cell_len`, embedding the `(N chars truncated — use full=true)` marker inline in the cell.

**Important gaps for a downstream consumer (verified, not guessed):**
- **No nested/table structures.** TOON itself (per `docs/spec/02-toon-format.md`'s grammar) is strictly one header + flat rows — no nested TOON blocks. In `toon::list()`, a struct field that's an array/object gets JSON-stringified and dropped into a single quoted cell (confirmed by test `list_stringifies_nested_values_losslessly`: `tags: Vec&lt;String&gt;` becomes cell value `"[\"a\",\"b\"]"`). There is no recursive/multi-table TOON renderer.
- **No parser in the public API**, despite the crate's own `Cargo.toml` description saying "renderer and parser." The only TOON parser in the repo is `tests/support/toon_parser.rs`, explicitly documented as "Test-only ... NOT part of the public API ... not a general-purpose TOON parser for untrusted input." `ARCHITECTURE.md`'s mention of a "`ToonSerializer`" type is also aspirational/inaccurate — no such type exists in `src/`.
- Row length is not validated by `render_toon()` itself — mismatches are silently rendered (missing cells → empty; the `#[cfg(debug_assertions)] debug_assert!` in `render.rs` would panic in debug builds, but that's dev-only). Call `.validate()` explicitly if you need hard errors.

**Dependencies on siblings:** depends on `michi-truncate` (for per-cell truncation). Depends on nothing else in the workspace.

---

## 2. `michi-truncate` (`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/crates/michi-truncate/src/lib.rs`)

**Purpose:** Truncate a string to at most N Unicode **scalar values (chars)** — not bytes, not model tokens — at a UTF-8 char boundary, appending a human/agent-readable marker describing what was cut and how to get the rest.

**Public API (the entire crate is ~90 lines of non-test code):**
```rust
pub struct Truncated {
    pub content: String,
    pub original_len: usize,      // original BYTE length (not char count)
    pub was_truncated: bool,
    pub signal: Option&lt;String&gt;,   // the marker text actually embedded in `content`
}

pub fn truncate(content: &amp;str, max_chars: usize, hint: &amp;str) -&gt; Truncated;
pub fn truncate_inline(content: &amp;str, max_chars: usize, hint: &amp;str) -&gt; String; // == truncate(...).content
```

**Algorithm (exact, confirmed by reading the code):**
- Fast-path: if `content.len() &lt;= max_chars` (byte length), return unchanged (byte length ≥ char count, so this implies char count also fits) — `was_truncated: false`, `signal: None`.
- Otherwise compute real `char_count`; if that's also `&lt;= max_chars`, still unchanged.
- Otherwise: build suffix `" ({char_count} chars truncated — use {hint})"`, compute how many content chars can be kept (`max_chars - suffix_chars`, saturating), slice at that char boundary via `char_indices().nth(...)`, append the suffix, then hard-cap the whole result to `max_chars` chars again in case the suffix alone is longer than `max_chars` (tiny `max_chars` or long `hint` string) — trims via char boundary, never mid-codepoint.
- `signal` is recomputed from what's *actually* left in `content` after the hard-cap (trimmed of leading space) — `None` if there was no room for any signal text, otherwise guaranteed to literally appear in `content`.

**Answering the task's specific questions:**
- Unit is **chars (Unicode scalars)**, not bytes and not LLM tokens — there is no tokenizer dependency anywhere in this crate or workspace (`Cargo.toml` has zero `[dependencies]`).
- Truncation marker: yes, `" ({n} chars truncated — use {hint})"`, caller-supplied `hint` string (e.g. `"full=true"`) is meant to tell the agent how to get the untruncated content (e.g., a param name to pass on the next call).
- Return value: `Truncated` struct with the content **and** metadata (`original_len`, `was_truncated`, `signal`) — not just a bare string. `truncate_inline()` is a convenience that discards the metadata and returns only the string (this is what `michi-toon`'s per-cell truncation calls).

**Cargo features:** none; `default = []`, zero dependencies.

**Note:** `PRINCIPLES.md` sketches a hypothetical future `TruncationOutcome&lt;T&gt;` / `truncate_by_count` / `truncate_by_bytes` API as a *worked example of design philosophy* ("split the decision from the signal") — **this is not implemented**. Don't mistake it for shipped API; the only truncation primitive that exists today is the char-based `truncate()`/`truncate_inline()` above.

**Dependencies on siblings:** none — this is the most upstream crate; everything else depends on it (directly or transitively).

---

## 3. `michi-resilience` (`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/crates/michi-resilience/src/lib.rs`)

**Purpose:** Pure math/parsing for retry decisions — **not** an actual retry executor, HTTP client wrapper, or circuit breaker. It computes "how long should I wait before retry N" and parses `Retry-After`; the caller still owns the actual retry loop.

**Public API:**
```rust
pub struct RetryConfig { pub max_retries: u32, pub base_delay: Duration, pub max_delay: Duration, pub jitter_factor: f64 } // #[non_exhaustive]
impl RetryConfig {
    pub fn new(max_retries, base_delay, max_delay, jitter_factor) -&gt; Self;        // clamps invalid inputs silently
    pub fn try_new(...) -&gt; Result&lt;Self, RetryConfigError&gt;;                        // strict, errors on invalid inputs
}
impl Default for RetryConfig; // max_retries=3, base_delay=500ms, max_delay=30s, jitter_factor=0.2

pub enum RetryConfigError { MaxDelayIsZero, BaseDelayExceedsMaxDelay{base,max}, JitterFactorOutOfRange{factor} }

pub fn next_retry_delay(config: &amp;RetryConfig, attempt: u32, jitter_seed: f64, retry_after: Option&lt;Duration&gt;) -&gt; Option&lt;Duration&gt;;
// None once attempt &gt;= max_retries. jitter_seed is caller-supplied random [0,1] — crate has no RNG dependency.
// Delay = max(exponential-backoff-with-jitter, retry_after), capped at max_delay.

pub fn parse_retry_after(header_value: &amp;str) -&gt; Option&lt;Duration&gt;;                  // RFC 7231 Retry-After: seconds or HTTP-date
pub fn parse_retry_after_at(header_value: &amp;str, now: SystemTime) -&gt; Option&lt;Duration&gt;; // testable variant, explicit "now"
pub fn is_retryable_status(status: u16) -&gt; bool;  // true only for 429, 502, 503, 504 (NOT 500 — deliberate, tested)

pub struct IdempotencyKey(pub String);
impl IdempotencyKey {
    pub fn new(s: impl Into&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn as_str(&amp;self) -&gt; &amp;str;
    pub fn from_hash(operation: &amp;str, data: &amp;[u8]) -&gt; Self;  // FNV-1a 64-bit hash, formatted "{operation}:{hash:016x}"
}
// From&lt;String&gt;, From&lt;&amp;str&gt; also implemented.

pub enum AlreadyDone { Yes { result: String }, No } // #[non_exhaustive]
pub fn already_done(stored: Option&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; AlreadyDone;
pub fn render_already_done(operation: &amp;str, summary: &amp;str, hints: &amp;[String]) -&gt; String;
```

**Cargo features:** none; `default = []`, zero dependencies at all (`[dependencies]` section is empty).

**Usage pattern:** caller supplies its own RNG for `jitter_seed`, its own HTTP client, and its own storage for idempotency lookups; this crate just does the arithmetic/parsing/formatting around those. `is_retryable_status` deliberately excludes 500 (documented reasoning in a test: "500 is a server bug — retrying reproduces it and risks duplicate side effects on writes").

**No circuit breaker, no async, no actual retry loop.** `docs/spec/06-decisions.md` confirms this is intentional: an async `CircuitBreaker` and retry-wrapper are explicitly deferred to a future, not-yet-built `pipeline` crate (which would depend on tokio) — they are called out as *not* belonging in `michi-resilience` because that crate must stay dependency-free and sync.

**Dependencies on siblings:** none.

---

## 4. `michi-core` (`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/crates/michi-core/src/`)

**Purpose:** The high-level composition layer — turns hints/errors/lists/kv-pairs/status into ready-to-print agent text or an MCP `CallToolResult`. This is the crate most directly reusable for a CLI/agent tool's output layer.

**Modules and API surface** (`crates/michi-core/src/lib.rs` re-exports everything flat too, and the root `michi` crate re-exports `michi-core`'s items flat again — so `michi::DomainError`, `michi_core::DomainError`, and `michi_core::error::DomainError` all resolve to the same type):

**`error.rs`** — the module the task asked to prioritize:
```rust
pub enum ErrorClass { User, Internal, Transient }  // #[non_exhaustive]
pub struct Sensitive&lt;T&gt;(pub T);  // Debug/Display both print "&lt;redacted&gt;" — for secrets in error context

pub enum ErrorCode { InvalidInput, NotFound, Unauthorized, Forbidden, Conflict, RateLimited, Unavailable, Timeout, ExternalFailure } // #[non_exhaustive]
impl ErrorCode {
    pub fn label(&amp;self) -&gt; &amp;'static str;               // snake_case label, e.g. "not_found"
    pub fn is_retryable_by_default(&amp;self) -&gt; bool;      // true for RateLimited/Unavailable/Timeout/ExternalFailure
    pub fn default_class(&amp;self) -&gt; ErrorClass;          // maps code -&gt; User or Internal
}

pub struct DomainError { pub code: ErrorCode, pub message: String, pub hints: Vec&lt;Hint&gt;, pub recovery: Option&lt;RecoveryHint&gt;, pub retryable: bool, pub retry_after: Option&lt;Duration&gt; } // #[non_exhaustive]
impl DomainError {
    pub fn new(code: ErrorCode, message: impl Into&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; Self;  // retryable defaults from code.is_retryable_by_default()
    pub fn hint(self, hint: impl Into&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; Self;               // builder, chainable
    pub fn recovery(self, r: RecoveryHint) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn retryable(self, retryable: bool) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn retry_after(self, d: Duration) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn exit_code(&amp;self) -&gt; i32;                     // always 1
    pub fn render(&amp;self) -&gt; String;                     // "error: &lt;label&gt;\nmessage: &lt;msg&gt;\nexit_code: 1\n" + help[]/recovery[] blocks
    pub fn render_github_annotation(&amp;self) -&gt; String;    // "::error title=&lt;label&gt;::&lt;msg&gt;" with \n/\r percent-encoded
    pub fn render_json(&amp;self) -&gt; String;                 // hand-rolled JSON: {"isError":true,"error":...,"message":...,"retryable":bool,"hints":[...],"recovery":null|{...}}
    pub fn to_call_tool_result(&amp;self) -&gt; CallToolResult;  // is_error:true, content = [render()], structured_content = render_json()
}
impl Display, std::error::Error for DomainError.
#[cfg(feature="miette")] impl miette::Diagnostic for DomainError  // ONLY implements code() and help() — no source spans, no derive, no labels/severity

pub enum Error { InvalidInput(String), NotFound(String), Domain(#[from] DomainError) }  // #[non_exhaustive], thiserror-derived
impl Error { pub fn render(&amp;self) -&gt; String; pub fn exit_code(&amp;self) -&gt; i32; pub fn class(&amp;self) -&gt; ErrorClass; pub fn is_retryable(&amp;self) -&gt; bool; }
```
Note: `render_json()`/`to_call_tool_result()` build JSON via hand-written string escaping (`crate::kv::json_escape_str`), not `serde_json` — works even without the `serde` feature.

**`mcp.rs`** — MCP integration, always compiled (no feature gate):
```rust
pub struct ContentBlock { pub text: String, pub audience: Vec&lt;Audience&gt; } // #[non_exhaustive]
pub struct CallToolResult { pub content: Vec&lt;ContentBlock&gt;, pub is_error: bool, pub structured_content: String } // #[non_exhaustive]
```
With `serde` feature: both get custom wire-shape mapping (`ContentBlockWire` renders `type: "text"`, `annotations: { audience }`; `CallToolResultWire` parses `structured_content` from a JSON string into a `serde_json::Value` and back) — this is the piece that would matter if monokl exposes an actual MCP tool endpoint.

**`response.rs`** — `AgentResponse`, the main builder, fluent/chainable, `#[non_exhaustive]`:
```rust
pub enum OutputFormat { #[default] Text, Json }
pub const DEFAULT_TRUNCATE_CELLS: usize = 200;
impl AgentResponse {
    pub fn new(type_name: impl Into&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn items(self, rows: Vec&lt;Vec&lt;Value&gt;&gt;, fields: &amp;[&amp;str]) -&gt; Self;   // sets TOON render path
    pub fn kv_items(self, items: Vec&lt;KvItem&gt;) -&gt; Self;                    // sets KV render path
    pub fn total_count(self, n: usize) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn hint(self, hint: impl Into&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn hints(self, hints: Vec&lt;Hint&gt;) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn recovery_hint(self, r: RecoveryHint) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn truncate_cells_at(self, limit: usize) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn as_error(self) -&gt; Self;
    pub fn human_content(self, text: impl Into&lt;String&gt;) -&gt; Self;         // dual assistant/user output
    pub fn render(&amp;self, format: OutputFormat) -&gt; String;
    pub fn render_toon(&amp;self) -&gt; String;
    pub fn render_kv(&amp;self) -&gt; String;
    pub fn render_hints_only(&amp;self) -&gt; String;
    pub fn render_for(&amp;self, audience: Audience) -&gt; String;              // Assistant -&gt; render_text(); User -&gt; human_content or fallback to text
    pub fn has_human_content(&amp;self) -&gt; bool;
    pub fn to_call_tool_result(&amp;self) -&gt; CallToolResult;
}
```
Note: `docs/spec/03-rust-api.md` documents these builder methods as taking `&amp;mut self`, but **the actual code in `response.rs` uses `self` (consuming, chainable) builder style** — the spec doc is stale here; trust the source.

**Other modules:** `audience.rs` (`enum Audience { Assistant, User }`), `hints.rs` (`struct Hint(pub String)`, `render_hints`/`append_hints` — produces `help[N]:\n  ...` blocks), `recovery.rs` (`struct RecoveryHint { tool, params: Vec&lt;(String,KvValue)&gt;, reason: Option&lt;String&gt; }`, `.param()`/`.reason()` builders, `render_recovery`/`append_recovery` — produces `recovery[N]:\n  tool: suggestedParams: { k: v } — reason` lines), `kv/mod.rs` (`struct KvItem{key,value}`, `enum KvValue{Text,Int,Float(f64,u8),Bool,Duration,Missing}`, `render_kv(items, total_count, hints) -&gt; String` — column-aligned `key: value` block, `Missing` renders as em-dash `—`), `status.rs` (`enum Health{Ok,Degraded(String),Error(String)}`, `StatusItem`, `StatusResponse::new/with_hints/render` — `tool:`/`description:` lead lines + per-item health annotations `[DEGRADED: ...]`/`[ERROR: ...]`), `idempotency.rs` (`FailedOp`, `PartialSuccess{completed,failed,skipped}.render()/.exit_code()` — for partially-completed multi-step ops, distinct from `michi-resilience::AlreadyDone` which is for fully-completed-before ops), `empty.rs` (`empty_state(type_name)`, `empty_state_with_hints` — `"type[0]{}:\ntotalCount: 0\n"`), `pipeline/mod.rs` (`PipelineStep`, `Pipeline{id,steps}.render()` — pure data + TOON-style render only; no execution, execution is deferred to the not-yet-built async pipeline crate), `telemetry/mod.rs` (`NoopProvider` — literally does nothing, `.span()`/`.count()` are no-ops; a zero-cost placeholder for future real telemetry).

**Cargo features:** `default = []`. `serde = ["dep:serde","dep:serde_json","michi-toon/serde"]` — needed for `Audience`/`Hint`/`KvValue`/`ContentBlock`/`CallToolResult` `Serialize`/`Deserialize` and the wire-shape mapping. `schemars = ["dep:schemars","michi-toon/schemars"]` — needed for `JsonSchema` derives (useful for MCP `outputSchema` generation) on most public structs. `miette = ["dep:miette"]` — needed only if you want `DomainError` to implement `miette::Diagnostic` (very thin: just `code()`+`help()`).

**Dependencies on siblings:** depends on `michi-truncate`, `michi-resilience`, and `michi-toon` (uses `michi_toon::escape_value`/`escape_value_quoted` in `idempotency.rs` and `pipeline/mod.rs`, and `michi_toon::Value`/`ToonOptions`/`render_toon` in `response.rs`). Also re-exports `michi_resilience::*` and `michi_truncate::*` at its own root (`pub use michi_resilience::*; pub use michi_truncate::*;` in `lib.rs`), so `michi_core::RetryConfig`, `michi_core::truncate`, etc. all resolve too.

## 5. Root `michi` facade (`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/src/lib.rs`)

Ten lines of real logic: `#![deny(unsafe_code)] #![warn(missing_docs)]`, then:
```rust
pub use michi_core::*;
pub use michi_resilience as resilience;
pub use michi_toon as toon;
pub use michi_truncate as truncate;
#[cfg(feature = "napi")] pub mod napi;
pub type Result&lt;T&gt; = std::result::Result&lt;T, michi_core::Error&gt;;
```
So everything from `michi-core` (which itself re-exports `michi-resilience` and `michi-truncate`) is available flat at `michi::*`, while `michi-toon` is namespaced under `michi::toon::*` only (not flattened at the root — confirmed by `tests/toon_integration.rs`'s `crate_root_reexports_are_reachable` test, which accesses `michi::render_kv`, `michi::Hint`, `michi::AlreadyDone`, `michi::RetryConfig`, `michi::Truncated`, `michi::truncate`/`truncate_inline` directly at root, but always reaches TOON types via `michi::toon::...`).

**Cargo features on the facade crate:** `default = []`. `napi = [...]` — Node.js bindings only, irrelevant to a Rust consumer like monokl. `serde`, `schemars`, `miette` — simple pass-throughs that turn on the corresponding sub-crate features.

`src/napi.rs` / `src/napi.rs/num.rs` and `packages/michi-node/` are the Node.js NAPI binding layer — not relevant to a Rust-only consumer; only compiled under the `napi` feature.

---

## Dependency graph among the five crates

```
michi-truncate   (zero deps — most upstream)
      ^
      |
michi-resilience (zero deps, no dependency on truncate or toon)
      ^
      |
michi-toon  ---depends on---&gt; michi-truncate
      ^
      |
michi-core  ---depends on---&gt; michi-truncate, michi-resilience, michi-toon
      ^
      |
michi (facade) ---depends on---&gt; all four above, re-exports them
```
Confirmed directly from each crate's `Cargo.toml [dependencies]` section: `michi-resilience` has zero deps (not even on `michi-truncate`); `michi-toon` depends only on `michi-truncate` + `compact_str`; `michi-core` depends on all three primitive crates + `thiserror`; the root `michi` crate depends on all four workspace crates by path (version `"0.1.0"` pins, so a git-tag dependency would need all four to be tagged together, which the single `v0.1.0` workspace tag already satisfies).

---

## Practical implications for monokl

1. **Reuse `michi-toon::{ToonOptions, Value, render_toon}` directly** for any tabular/list agent output monokl needs — it's a complete, tested implementation matching the format monokl's spec presumably already targets (michi's own `docs/spec/02-toon-format.md` is the TOON grammar). Don't hand-roll TOON escaping/truncation — `escape_value`, `sanitize_header_token` and the max-cell-len truncation logic already handle the edge cases (commas, quotes, newlines, NaN/Inf floats, structural chars in headers).
2. **`michi-core::response::AgentResponse`** is the highest-leverage single reuse point if monokl wants a unified builder for TOON-list-or-KV output plus hints/recovery/error/MCP-result assembly — it composes nearly everything else in the workspace.
3. **`michi-core::error::DomainError`** is a ready-made typed error model (code/class/hints/recovery/retryable/retry_after) with three render targets (agent text, GitHub Actions annotation, JSON) and an MCP `CallToolResult` conversion — likely reusable wholesale rather than reinventing monokl's own error-rendering.
4. **`michi-truncate::truncate`** truncates by **character count**, not tokens or bytes — if monokl needs actual LLM-token-budget truncation, this crate does not provide that; it would need to be layered on top (e.g., feed a token-estimated char budget into `truncate()`).
5. **`michi-resilience`** gives retry-delay math and `Retry-After` parsing only — if monokl needs an actual retry-executing HTTP wrapper or a circuit breaker, that does not exist yet anywhere in this workspace (explicitly deferred to a future `pipeline` crate per `docs/spec/06-decisions.md`).
6. **No TOON parser exists as public API** — if monokl needs to *read* TOON (not just emit it), that would have to be written new; michi does not supply it despite package descriptions implying otherwise.
7. **Not yet on crates.io** — monokl would depend via git tag (`michi = { git = "https://github.com/orin-axi/michi", tag = "v0.1.0" }`, plus corresponding `michi-core`/`michi-toon`/etc. path deps are internal so a single top-level `michi` git dep should suffice) per the project's own stated interim recommendation, and per `PRINCIPLES.md`, monokl adopting it for real would itself count as the "real consumer integration" that's gating the eventual crates.io release.</result>

<result>## Findings: monokl spec corpus vs. shared "agent-ergonomic output primitives" (michi)

Read all 7 files in full (`01`–`07`, ~9,190 lines). One meta-finding first: **michi is never discussed as a design option anywhere in the corpus.** The string "michi" appears exactly once, at `02-inspection-and-analysis.md:1713`, only as a workspace-crate-name literal inside an import-classifier allowlist (`monokl`/`michi`/`firkin`/`lumen`/`pulse`/...) — recognizing michi as a sibling crate for import-bucketing purposes, not as a dependency for output rendering. "TOON" appears zero times. `05-research-and-decisions.md`, despite being an exhaustive 200-line decision log covering SCIP, LSP, ast-grep, difftastic, embedding rerank, and more, never once raises "should our output/error/truncation layer be a shared crate" as a question — this exact question has had no research pass at all.

---

### 1. Output / response rendering

- **`01-core-architecture.md:2401-2441`** — `output.rs` v1: `render_json`/`render_json_compact`/`render_output` hand-roll the pretty-vs-compact JSON decision via `std::io::stdout().is_terminal()`; `fatal()` and `render_error()` are hand-rolled stdout/stderr exit paths.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:2436-2441`** — `render_error` builds a JSON error envelope via raw string interpolation: `println!("{{\"error\": \"{err}\"}}")` — not structured serialization, a hand-built JSON string.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:2525`** — Explicit self-acknowledged inconsistency: the inline `TokenCountResult` in `main.rs` uses snake_case (`total_tokens`) while every other response type in the system is camelCase. A shared output-serialization layer enforcing one casing convention would prevent this by construction instead of relying on per-struct `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]` discipline.
- **`03-multi-language-platform.md:2839-2933`** ("`output.rs` — updated rendering") — A large hand-rolled presentation-resolution engine: `ResolvedProfile{Agent,Human}`, `ResolvedFormat{Json,Minimal,Text,Mermaid,Schema}`, `Presentation{profile,detail,format,use_color,pretty_json}`, `RenderKind{Structured,RankedList,Summary,Symbols,Graph}`, and `resolve_presentation()` (lines 2864-2909) resolving TTY/agent-vs-human/format/color from five overlapping flags. Plus ~10 per-command hand-written text renderers (`render_search_text`, `render_symbols_text`, `render_dependents_mermaid`, etc., lines 2912-2922) and ANSI color helpers (`human_heading`/`human_score`/etc., lines 2924-2928) built on `owo-colors`. This is a full bespoke "agent output mode vs. human output mode" resolution layer — exactly the shape of thing a shared output-primitives crate would centralize once four tools (monokl/Firkin/Lumen/Pulse) each need "is this call an agent or a human, and how verbose should the answer be."
- **`03-multi-language-platform.md:1570-1749`** (`projection.rs`) — A complete hand-rolled JSON field-projection/pruning system: `project_fields` (dot-notation field selection), `project_search_response`/`project_inspect_result` (per-command field allowlists with hardcoded aliasing `"snippet"→"code"`, `"score"→"finalScore"`), and `prune_empty` (drops null/false/0/empty-string/empty-array/empty-object recursively — the entire implementation of `--format minimal`). The module doc comment itself says: *"CLI and MCP surfaces both need compact, question-shaped output. Projection lives in the library so those surfaces can share one contract"* (line 1576) — this is monokl explicitly building its own version of exactly the shared-output-contract concern michi is meant to own.
- **`03-multi-language-platform.md:2990-3018`** ("Agent tool contracts") — Monokl's own from-scratch "agent contract" spec: JSON-as-canonical-output policy, field projection, five output-format modes, capability-driven diagnostics, truncation markers, scope flags. This section is monokl inventing its own version of a cross-tool agent-output contract that a shared michi layer would otherwise standardize once across the whole orin-axi suite.
- **`02-inspection-and-analysis.md:928-976`** ("`mnkl schema`") — A fully hand-written JSON Schema generator ("Hand-written (no `schemars`)"), ~30 per-type schema-builder functions (`react_component_schema()`, `rust_struct_schema()`, etc., enumerated at line 976) built via `serde_json::json!` macros rather than derived from the actual Rust types. The corpus even has a dedicated drift-detection test (`schema_keys_match_serialized_instance`, `02-inspection-and-analysis.md:1889-1896`) whose sole purpose is catching divergence between this hand-maintained schema and the real serialized output — a problem a shared, type-derived schema mechanism would eliminate structurally instead of testing for.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:2587-2665`** — Section 24 snapshot dump shows the canonical JSON key sets per command (search/symbols/dependents/count-tokens) hand-maintained via `insta` snapshots rather than a shared schema/output-shape contract.

### 2. Error rendering / diagnostic presentation

- **`01-core-architecture.md:190-252`** — `MonoklError` enum: `thiserror` `#[error(...)]` strings per variant, optional `#[cfg_attr(feature = "miette", derive(miette::Diagnostic))]`. No hint/help/recoverability field on any variant — no structured "is this transient/retryable" or "here's what to do" data, only free-text `Display`.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:228`** — `StaleDiskCache`'s error text references `mnkl init --rebuild`, a CLI subcommand that doesn't exist anywhere in the spec (confirmed dead/wrong string, flagged again at `07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:38`). A shared, golden-tested error-message layer would likely catch this by construction.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:2445-2523` + `07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:54`** — **The most direct finding.** Two contradictory, un-reconciled error-output paths both exist: `output.rs::render_error` (line 2436) defines a JSON-on-stdout envelope with `exit(1)`, but `main()` (Section 22) never calls it — every command instead propagates errors through `miette::Result&lt;()&gt;` via `.into_diagnostic().wrap_err(...)`, which renders miette's fancy human-readable diagnostic to stderr. Direct quote from the edge-cases doc: *"The spec doesn't say which governs — a real problem for a tool whose value proposition is agent-parseable output."* This is precisely the kind of error-to-agent-output inconsistency a single shared error-rendering path (michi-resilience / michi-core) would prevent by only offering one canonical mechanism.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:2892-2946`** (napi-support `boundary.rs`) — `flatten_error()` (lines 2924-2932) hand-rolls error-cause-chain flattening: `format!("{err}")` plus a manual `while let Some(s) = source` loop writing `"\n  caused by: {s}"` for each link — a from-scratch reimplementation of what a shared diagnostic-chain renderer (or even `anyhow`) already does.
- **`01-core-architecture.md:490-504` and `02-inspection-and-analysis.md:169-184`** — `Diagnostic{kind: DiagnosticKind, path, message}` / `DiagnosticKind{Degraded,Skipped,Warning}` is defined twice (once per doc part), a free-text-message, enum-kind "soft hint" mechanism used everywhere for non-fatal warnings. It's structurally similar to what a shared hint/diagnostic type in michi-core would provide, but here it's monokl's own bespoke type with hand-written `format!()` message strings scattered per call site.
- **`03-multi-language-platform.md:1751-1980`** (`query_support.rs`) — `add_precision_diagnostic`, `add_missing_analyzer_diagnostic`, `require_precision_capability`, `precision_label`, `language_label` are a full bespoke "diagnostic message templating" subsystem, each hand-formatting strings like `"{operation} is not supported for {lang} files in this build"` and `"{op} uses {actual}-precision {lang} analysis"` per capability-check call site.
- **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:71`** — *"Nearly every Part 2 command declares a `diagnostics: Vec&lt;Diagnostic&gt;` field but never describes it being populated"* — systemic under-specification of the hint/diagnostic mechanism across `refs`, `definition`, `diff`, `tokens`, `explain`, `coverage`, `data-flow`, `similar`.

### 3. Truncation / budget logic

- **`01-core-architecture.md:1228-1288`** (`budget.rs`) — Hand-rolled `tiktoken-rs`/`o200k_base` wrapper (`tokenizer()`, `count_tokens()`, lines 1231-1246) and `apply_budget()` (lines 1248-1286): a manual loop capping `max_results`/`max_bytes`/`max_tokens` sequentially and constructing a truncation-marker string via `format!("[+{omitted} more results omitted; refine with +term or -term]")` (line 1274). This is exactly the "cap output size, note what's omitted" concern michi-truncate is presumably built for.
- **Three separately hand-written truncation-marker string formats**, one per command, no shared template:
  - search: `"[+{omitted} more results omitted; refine with +term or -term]"` (`01-core-architecture.md:1274`)
  - symbols: `"[symbol cap 500 reached; pass fewer files or increase limit]"` (`01-core-architecture.md:2330`)
  - dependents: `"[results capped at 200; {tot_dep} dependents, {tot_imp} imports total]"` (`01-core-architecture.md:2332`, restated at `03-multi-language-platform.md:1174`)
  This is a direct instance of "we invented our own version of X" three separate times for the same underlying concern.
- **`05-research-and-decisions.md:24`** — Proposes extending `MonoklError` with a new `TraversalCapExceeded{visited, cap}` variant and a `TraversalReport{sites, truncated}` wrapper "rather than silently truncating a bare `Vec`," and explicitly notes *"the same bounded-BFS pattern generalizes to any of this spec's existing traversals... that don't yet have explicit cap enforcement at every site"* — i.e., monokl's own research log recognizes the cap/truncate pattern needs to be applied consistently but has no shared primitive to reach for, so it's reinventing it per traversal.
- **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:23`** (cross-cutting pattern) — *"Resource limits are opt-out by construction. Only `max_bytes` is clamped to a hard ceiling; `max_results`, `max_tokens`, `max_candidates`, query string length, per-file hit counts, git change-set size, and `ImpactedNeighbors` expansion are all caller-controlled with no enforced cap"* — restated per-finding at lines 43, 51-52, 118-120. A shared budget/truncation primitive that structurally requires every result-producing path to declare and enforce a cap would prevent this whole class of gap.
- **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:129`** — *"The 50MB `io_safety` cap and the 2MB search token/byte budget coexist with no explained relationship — a 49MB file passes the safety gate, gets fully parsed, then has virtually all output discarded by the budget."* Two independently invented size-cap systems (`io_safety.rs`'s read-size cap vs. `budget.rs`'s token/byte cap) that don't compose — a symptom of not having one shared budget abstraction spanning read-time and output-time limits.
- **`06-daemon-architecture.md:163`** — The `PrecisionLedger` design deliberately keeps a compact `provenance` tag on every result by default, with full reasoning available only on demand via `explain` ("Default output stays terse; depth is opt-in.") — a progressive-disclosure output pattern invented ad hoc for this one subsystem rather than drawn from a shared primitive.

### 4. Resilience / retry-shaped logic

- **`01-core-architecture.md:3129`** — The LSP architecture description includes a genuine hand-rolled circuit breaker: *"`LspSession`... circuit breaker — 3 crashes in 60s → 'tripped', 500ms backoff."* This is real retry/backoff-shaped logic and a strong candidate for a shared michi-resilience primitive rather than a bespoke per-subsystem implementation.
- **`06-daemon-architecture.md`** §9 "Known gaps" (lines 242-250) plus the corresponding edge-cases findings are the richest resilience-gap cluster:
  - **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:163`** — *"`Session(Owned)` resources have no orphan-detection or crash-recovery story, and no concurrent-ownership arbitration."*
  - **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:164`** — *"`PrecisionUpgrader::upgrade()` failing mid-session has no defined degrade-back-to-baseline contract... No fallback-to-cached-baseline, no re-establishment story, no circuit-breaker against crash-looping."* This explicitly names the missing piece as a circuit breaker.
  - **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:165`** — Kotlin/Scala's JVM-subprocess tier has no fallback-when-absent story, unlike the parallel C/C++ Clang-absent fallback — inconsistent degrade-policy across backends, i.e., no single shared "how do we fail over" primitive being reused.
- **`06-daemon-architecture.md:184`** (§6 rule 4) — *"When only generic LSP is available and no sentinel exists, cap at `Structural`... and emit a monokl `Diagnostic` explaining the downgrade rather than guessing"* — a degrade-and-explain pattern, resilience-adjacent, hand-specified per backend rather than via a shared transient-vs-permanent-failure primitive.
- **`07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:34, 36-37`** — Cache-layer resilience gaps built entirely bespoke: no panic/stack-overflow guard around the parser in the main pipeline (unlike the napi boundary, which does wrap with `catch_unwind`, `01-core-architecture.md:2900-2932`); concurrent disk-cache writes race last-writer-wins; a genuinely corrupted `cache.json` hard-fails instead of self-healing (only the "stale version" case self-heals). The `03-multi-language-platform.md:998-1022` `fs2` advisory-lock fix (`with_cache_lock`) is monokl's own hand-rolled cross-process coordination primitive addressing part of this — another instance of building infra-level resilience machinery locally rather than drawing on a shared crate.

### 5. Other agent-ergonomics concerns

- **JSON key casing inconsistency** — `01-core-architecture.md:2525, 2646-2665`: `count_tokens` result uses snake_case (`total_tokens`, `tokens`, `path`) while every other command's output is camelCase (confirmed via the Section 24 snapshot dump, e.g. `search_top_level_keys` includes `totalTokens`/`truncationMarker`). A shared serialization convention would prevent this by construction.
- **Field-alias mapping hand-written per command** — `03-multi-language-platform.md:1658-1690` (`search_projection_fields`): `"snippet" =&gt; "code"`, `"score" =&gt; "finalScore"` is a bespoke, unenforced alias table; a shared projection/alias primitive (declarative rather than a per-command `match`) would generalize this instead of requiring a new hand-written function per new command (`inspect_projection_fields` at line 1692 duplicates the same shape for a different field set).
- **Hint/next-step text embedded as raw strings, not structured fields** — the search truncation marker (`01-core-architecture.md:1274`) tells the agent what to do next ("refine with +term or -term") entirely inside a free-text `Option&lt;String&gt;` rather than a structured "suggestion" type; likewise `dependents`' tsconfig-missing warning is a `Warning`-kind `Diagnostic` whose only machine-readable content is a message string containing the literal text `"tsconfig.json"` (`01-core-architecture.md:2332`). This is the "what to do next" agent-ergonomics concern being solved with ad hoc prose instead of a structured hint type.
- **Truncation/diagnostic/output-format design decisions have had zero cross-referencing against a shared library anywhere in the "decision log" doc** — worth restating as the top-line finding: `05-research-and-decisions.md` extensively researches and either adopts or rejects prior art for ranking, resolution, parsing, and eval methodology (SCIP, stack-graphs, ast-grep, difftastic, CHA/RTA/k-CFA, embedding rerank, etc.) but at no point asks whether monokl's own output/truncation/error/diagnostic machinery (Sections 1, 3, 4, 5 above) should be delegated to the sibling `michi` crate instead of being independently designed and re-derived per command. Given the project's own `CLAUDE.md` states monokl is supposed to depend on michi for exactly this ("agent-facing output rendering... structured errors, hints... rather than reinventing it"), this is a real gap in the spec's own research discipline, not just an implementation-detail oversight.

### Summary of highest-value dedupe candidates

If a decision pass is warranted before more of this gets implemented, the four clearest candidates to replace with michi (in order of how much duplicated/inconsistent logic they'd eliminate) are:

1. **`projection.rs`** (`03-multi-language-platform.md:1570-1749`) + **`output.rs`'s format/profile resolution** (`03-multi-language-platform.md:2839-2933`) — the field-projection, `--format minimal` pruning, and agent-vs-human presentation-resolution logic is the single largest chunk of hand-rolled "agent-ergonomic output primitive" code in the corpus.
2. **Truncation markers** (`01-core-architecture.md:1274, 2330, 2332`) — three inconsistent hand-written formats for the same "capped, here's what's missing" concern.
3. **Error rendering path** (`01-core-architecture.md:2436-2441` vs. `2445-2523`, cross-referenced at `07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md:54`) — the dead-vs-wired JSON-vs-miette conflict is a concrete bug that a single shared error-to-agent-output path would have made structurally impossible.
4. **Diagnostic/hint message templating** (`query_support.rs`, `03-multi-language-platform.md:1751-1980`) — repeated hand-written `format!()` diagnostic strings that a shared structured-hint type would replace.</result>

<result># michi ↔ monokl integration: architectural analysis

**Verdict: adapter-at-the-boundary, `cli`-feature-gated, michi types confined to one new module. JSON stays canonical and michi-free. TOON becomes an additive locator format, never the carrier for `code`.** Everything below is empirically verified against real michi source (`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi`) — I built and ran probes rather than reasoning from the API surface.

---

## 0. Three practical blockers that outrank the type design

These were not in the brief and change the sequencing answer.

**License.** `/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/Cargo.toml` declares `license = "AGPL-3.0-or-later"`. `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/Cargo.toml` declares `license = "MIT"` — but `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/LICENSE` **is already the AGPL text**. So monokl's manifest is self-inconsistent today, independent of michi. Linking michi resolves toward AGPL, which matches monokl's actual LICENSE file. Fix: change monokl's `license` field to `"AGPL-3.0-or-later"`. Bookkeeping, not a blocker — but it must be deliberate.

**MSRV.** michi declares `rust-version = "1.96"`; monokl declares `"1.85"`. Depending on michi silently makes monokl's declaration false. Local toolchain is `rustc 1.96.0`, so it builds here, but the manifest must be bumped (or michi lowered). michi is also `edition = "2021"` / `resolver = "2"` vs monokl's `edition = "2024"` / `resolver = "3"` — that mix is fine, editions are per-crate.

**Registry.** michi is path-deps-only, unpublished. monokl already consumes `io-errors` from the private `orin-cargo` registry — so publishing michi to `orin-cargo` is the consistent choice. **But**: `crates/io-errors/` does not exist and there is no `.cargo/config.toml`, so `cargo metadata` currently fails at manifest parse (`registry index was not found in any configuration: orin-cargo`). SPEC-004 AC-008B locks this as expected. **Consequence: you cannot empirically verify a michi dependency until that is resolved** — which matters because this project's process requires empirical toolchain verification.

---

## 1. Error model fit

### 1a. Variant-by-variant mapping

Using SPEC-001's locked Display strings (including the two corrections: `Json` → `"json parsing or serialization failed"`, `StaleDiskCache` → `"disk cache is stale (version or config hash mismatch)"`).

| MonoklError variant | `ErrorCode` | Fit | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Io(FileIoError)` | fan-out | **needs inspection** | `NotFound`/`PermissionDenied`/else → `NotFound`/`Forbidden`/`ExternalFailure`. One variant → 3 codes. |
| `Walk{path, source: ignore::Error}` | fan-out | **needs inspection** | `ignore::Error::Glob` is user-fault → `InvalidInput`; `Io` → per-kind; else `ExternalFailure`. |
| `RegexBuild(grep_regex::Error)` | `InvalidInput` | **clean** | |
| `NonUtf8Path{path}` | `InvalidInput` | **forced** | Not caller input — a file found on disk. No good home. |
| `TooManyTerms{count, limit}` | `InvalidInput` | **clean + gain** | Structured fields become a `RecoveryHint`. |
| `TokenizerInit` | `ExternalFailure` | **forced** | Internal init failure. No `Internal`/`Bug` code exists. |
| `Json(serde_json::Error)` | `ExternalFailure` | **forced** | Serializing monokl's *own* output — an internal bug. |
| `PathOutsideRoot{path}` | `Forbidden` | **clean** | Genuinely a boundary refusal. |
| `StaleDiskCache` | `Conflict` | **clean** | "Resource state mismatch" is exactly right. |
| `InvalidGitRef{ref_, reason}` | fan-out | **needs inspection** | The three locked `reason` values are all `InvalidInput`; a missing ref would be `NotFound`. |
| `Git{operation, message}` | `ExternalFailure` | **clean** | git *is* an external process. |
| `SymlinkRejected{path}` | `Forbidden` | **clean** | Same family as `PathOutsideRoot`. |
| `FileTooLarge{path, size, cap}` | `InvalidInput` | **forced** | HTTP 413 has no michi analogue. |
| `LockPoisoned{context}` | `ExternalFailure` | **forced** | Internal invariant violation. |

Tally: **6 clean, 3 fan-out, 5 forced.** Three semantically unrelated variants (`TokenizerInit`, `Json`, `LockPoisoned`) collapse onto `external_failure` — an agent reading only the code cannot distinguish "tokenizer failed to init" from "a lock was poisoned," which warrant different responses.

### 1b. The retry hazard — the real finding, not the label collapse

`ErrorCode::is_retryable_by_default()` returns `true` for `RateLimited | Unavailable | Timeout | ExternalFailure`, and `DomainError::new` **applies it automatically**. monokl is a local, synchronous, filesystem tool: essentially nothing it does is transiently retryable. Every one of the five variants routed to `ExternalFailure` would be born `retryable: true`, telling an agent to re-issue an identical call that will fail identically. **The adapter must call `.retryable(false)` unconditionally** — this is a correctness requirement, not hygiene.

### 1c. Information loss — the `#[source]` chain is the real casualty

`impl std::error::Error for DomainError {}` is empty, so `source()` returns `None`. The chain is not just flattened, it is *absent*. Worse: thiserror's `Display` does **not** include `#[source]`. So `DomainError::new(code, err.to_string())` on `MonoklError::Walk` emits only `"walker error at src/"` — the `ignore::Error` root cause vanishes silently. Same for `Io(FileIoError)` and its `io::Error`.

Fix (verified working): walk `Error::source()`, use line 1 as `message`, and push each remaining line as a separate `Hint`. This preserves everything *and* solves a second problem — see below.

### 1d. Two live injection bugs, one in each codebase

`DomainError::render()` writes `message` raw into a `key: value` block, and `michi_core::hints::append_hints` writes hints raw into `help[N]:` (unlike the TOON path, which sanitizes via `sanitize_hint`). Verified:

```
error: external_failure
message: git_failed: git rev-parse failed: fatal: ambiguous argument 'nope'
Use '--' to separate paths          ← un-keyed orphan line; breaks KV parsing
exit_code: 1
```

`MonoklError::Git{message}` carries git stderr, which is routinely multi-line. Unix paths may also contain `\n`. **The adapter owns line-discipline normalization.** The first-line-plus-hints split handles it correctly (verified output in §4).

Separately, monokl's own `output.rs::render_error` interpolates `{err}` raw into a JSON string literal — and `NonUtf8Path` / `InvalidGitRef` format with `{:?}`, emitting embedded quotes. That produces syntactically invalid JSON. Dead today, latent if ever wired.

### 1e. Two things michi genuinely *adds* that I did not expect

**miette gets stronger, not weaker.** SPEC-001 AC-002B locks `&lt;MonoklError as miette::Diagnostic&gt;::code()`, `::help()`, and `::url()` as returning `None` for all 14 variants — the derive exists "solely so downstream code compiles against the trait bound." michi's `impl miette::Diagnostic for DomainError` supplies `code()` (the label) and `help()` (joined hints). Routing the miette path through `DomainError` is a strict **upgrade** over monokl's deliberately inert surface.

**`RecoveryHint` restores information SPEC-001 deleted.** The `StaleDiskCache` correction stripped `"run \`mnkl init --rebuild\`"` from the Display string. That advice now has no home — except `RecoveryHint::new("init").param("rebuild", KvValue::Bool(true))`, which is exactly what it's for.

### 1f. Design: named trait, no `From`, monokl code carried in the message

Do **not** replace `MonoklError`. AGENTS.md line 18 is binding: *"One thiserror error enum per crate… don't add a String-message variant when a structured one is possible."* `DomainError` is precisely a String-message type — it is a *rendering target*, not a domain type.

I verified `impl From&lt;MonoklError&gt; for DomainError` **does** pass the orphan rule (local type in the trait's parameter position). But I recommend **against** providing it: `From` makes the conversion reachable by `?` anywhere in the crate, which defeats boundary containment. A named trait is greppable and cannot be triggered implicitly. It also accepts context (`command`) that `From` structurally cannot — and `RecoveryHint.tool` needs it.

To recover the collapsed distinctions, carry a monokl-specific code as a **message prefix** (`monokl_code()`). It survives every michi render path — `render()`, `render_json()`, `render_github_annotation()`, `Display` — with no michi change and no extra field. Verified: `"message":"too_many_terms: too many query terms: 41 (limit 32)"`.

---

## 2. Output model fit — TOON is disqualified for `search`

### 2a. `escape_value` does not escape newlines. It deletes them.

`/Users/gabe/Projects/michi/crates/michi-toon/src/escape.rs:17,33` — `'\n' | '\r' =&gt; {}`. Not `\\n`. Dropped. Verified end-to-end:

Input:
```ts
// authenticate the user
export async function login(u: string, p: string) {
```
TOON cell:
```
"// authenticate the userexport async function login(u: string, p: string) {  const t = await api.post('/login', { u, p });  return t.token;}"
```

**The entire function is now inside a `//` line comment.** This is not a readability complaint — it is semantic corruption of the exact payload monokl exists to deliver. There is **no configuration that avoids it**: `truncate_cells_at(usize::MAX)` still strips. And the KV path is identical — `kv/mod.rs:37` strips `\n`/`\r` too. **No michi text-render path can carry a multi-line string intact.**

### 2b. `toon::list()` silently drops fields — on exactly monokl's gated types

Field names come from the **first** item's serialized keys. With `skip_serializing_if`, a `None` in row 1 erases the column for every row. Verified:

```
=== row1 has None (key skipped), row2 has Some ===
blocks[2]{file,lineStart,matchedKeywords}:
  a.ts,1,"[\"x\"]"
  b.ts,2,[]                    ← symbolSignature "fn b()" SILENTLY GONE

=== reverse order ===
blocks[2]{file,lineStart,symbolSignature,matchedKeywords}:
  b.ts,2,fn b(),[]             ← same data, different schema
```

This lands on the gated types directly: SPEC-006 AC-002 puts `skip_serializing_if = "Vec::is_empty"` on `CodeBlock.matched_lines`/`matched_keywords`; SPEC-003 puts `skip_serializing_if = "Option::is_none"` on `SymbolEntry.owner`/`trait_impl`/`visibility`/`kind_detail`. It violates two PRINCIPLES.md tenets at once: *"a truncation marker, not silent data loss"* and *"the same query against the same code should produce byte-identical JSON"* — here the output **schema** depends on result ordering, which depends on BM25 scores.

Also: `toon::list()` column order depends on `serde_json`'s `preserve_order` feature being unified on (verified — monokl enables it; without it keys sort alphabetically). An implicit cross-crate feature dependency for a load-bearing determinism guarantee. **Do not use `toon::list()`. Use an explicit field list.**

### 2c. Three more michi behaviors the adapter must defend against

- `render_toon` **`debug_assert!`s** on row/header arity mismatch (`render.rs:142`) — verified panic. monokl's clippy config denies `panic`; this one comes from inside a dependency and would abort test builds.
- `AgentResponse::toon_body()` swallows `validate()` failure into the **body**: `"error: toon_validation_failed\nmessage: …"`, exit code still 0. Data silently replaced by an error string.
- Default `max_cell_len` is 200 and truncates with a **hardcoded** hint `full=true` — a flag monokl doesn't have. And the number is the *original* char count, not the omitted count.

### 2d. `OutputFormat::Json` is not JSON output

`AgentResponse::render(OutputFormat::Json)` produces `{"body":"&lt;the TOON text as a string&gt;","hints":[],"recovery":[],"isError":false}`. It is a JSON *envelope around TOON text*, not a structured serialization. **michi cannot carry monokl's `--format json` contract.** That contract is already gated (SPEC-006/007) with exact top-level key-set snapshots, and doc 03 §5 already states the governing rule: *"JSON remains the canonical agent contract."*

### 2e. Verdict, per output type

- **`search` / `RankedBlock`: TOON is disqualified** as a carrier for `code`. But it is excellent as a **locator table** — `file,lineStart,lineEnd,nodeKind,symbolSignature,rank,finalScore` plus a hint pointing at monokl's existing `extract --file --line-start --line-end`. That is textbook progressive disclosure, it is dramatically cheaper in tokens, and it turns michi's constraint into the right design rather than a workaround.
- **`symbols` / `SymbolEntry`: TOON fits well** — all scalars, and `escape_value` correctly quotes signatures containing commas (`HashMap&lt;K, V&gt;`, parameter lists). One transform needed: `SymbolsResult.files` is a `BTreeMap&lt;Utf8PathBuf, Vec&lt;SymbolEntry&gt;&gt;`, so the projection must flatten to one row per (file, symbol) with `file` as a leading column. **This is why the trait belongs on the response, not the row** — flattening is a container-level operation, so a per-item `ToToonRow` is the wrong abstraction.
- `truncation_marker` decomposes naturally: the *number* becomes michi's `totalCount` (row count vs. total tells the agent what's missing with no marker string at all), the *advice* becomes a `Hint`.
- Minor: `Value::Null` and `Value::Str("")` both render as an empty cell — `Some("")` and `None` are indistinguishable on the TOON wire.

---

## 3. Truncation fit

Genuinely different problems. michi-truncate: **char**-count, **single string**, marker `" ({n} chars truncated — use {hint})"` where *n* is the **original** length. monokl's budget.rs: **BPE token** count (`o200k_base`), over a **list**, with three interacting caps (`max_results` / `max_bytes` on `code` only / `max_tokens`), break-not-continue semantics, and a marker `"[+{omitted} more results omitted; refine with +term or -term]"` where the number is the **omitted** count.

**What can genuinely be reused:**
1. `truncate_inline()` as a **per-field safety net** — a single oversized `Diagnostic.message`, a pathological `symbol_signature`. Char-based is fine for a 200-char cap. This is real, direct reuse.
2. If monokl wants a hint other than `full=true` on the TOON path, it must pre-truncate with `truncate_inline(s, n, "detail=full")` and set `truncate_cells_at(usize::MAX)`. Also real reuse.

**What cannot:** anything in budget.rs. Token counting, list-level capping, the 2 MB ceiling, the omitted count — none of it exists in michi, and char count is not a proxy for BPE tokens on code.

**The `Truncated` struct shape:** worth noting that monokl **already independently arrived at it**. `Truncated { content, original_len, was_truncated, signal }` ≅ `SearchResponse { results, total_blocks_before_truncation, truncated, truncation_marker }`. The alignment is confirmatory, not actionable — no change needed.

**Marker wording:** do **not** retrofit michi's convention onto the JSON markers. They're gated and snapshot-tested; the gain is cosmetic. If a TOON surface is added, use michi's convention *there*, so each surface is internally consistent. One divergence to keep apart: michi's number is the original total, monokl's is the omitted count — if they ever co-occur in one payload an agent could misread them.

---

## 4. Recommended architecture (compiled and run)

michi enters through **one module**: `crates/monokl/src/output/agent.rs`, gated by `cli`. The gated lib.rs already has `#[cfg(feature = "cli")] pub mod output;`, so this needs **no lib.rs change** — `output.rs` becomes `output/mod.rs` + `output/agent.rs`, and `output.rs` is not itself a gated track (Track 21 is "Not started"). Manifest:

```toml
michi = { version = "0.1", registry = "orin-cargo", optional = true, features = ["miette"] }
cli   = ["dep:clap", "dep:michi", "miette"]
```

The library core (`budget`, `query`, `rank`, `types`, `error`, …) stays michi-free by construction.

### The TOON projection trait — arity in the type

```rust
/// `N` is the column count. Encoding it in the type makes a row/header arity
/// mismatch a *compile* error rather than michi-toon's `debug_assert!` panic
/// (michi-toon/src/render.rs:142).
pub trait ToonProjection&lt;const N: usize&gt; {
    const TYPE_NAME: &amp;'static str;
    const FIELDS: [&amp;'static str; N];
    fn toon_rows(&amp;self) -&gt; Vec&lt;[Value; N]&gt;;
    fn toon_total_count(&amp;self) -&gt; Option&lt;usize&gt; { None }
    fn toon_hints(&amp;self) -&gt; Vec&lt;Hint&gt; { Vec::new() }
}

pub fn to_agent_response&lt;const N: usize, P: ToonProjection&lt;N&gt;&gt;(p: &amp;P) -&gt; AgentResponse {
    let fields: Vec&lt;&amp;str&gt; = P::FIELDS.to_vec();
    let rows: Vec&lt;Vec&lt;Value&gt;&gt; = p.toon_rows().into_iter().map(|r| r.to_vec()).collect();
    let mut r = AgentResponse::new(P::TYPE_NAME)
        .items(rows, &amp;fields)
        .truncate_cells_at(usize::MAX);   // monokl owns truncation; michi must not silently cut
    if let Some(n) = p.toon_total_count() { r = r.total_count(n); }
    r.hints(p.toon_hints())
}

/// The ONLY sanctioned way to build a string cell. michi-toon's `escape_value`
/// DELETES `\n`/`\r` (escape.rs:17,33), concatenating code lines so a `//`
/// comment swallows the next line. Pre-escape to a literal two-char sequence.
pub fn cell(s: &amp;str) -&gt; Value {
    if s.contains('\n') || s.contains('\r') {
        Value::from(s.replace('\r', "").replace('\n', "\\n"))
    } else { Value::from(s) }
}
pub fn opt_cell(s: Option&lt;&amp;str&gt;) -&gt; Value { s.map_or(Value::Null, cell) }
```

`const N` as a *trait* parameter (not an associated const) is what makes this work on stable — `[&amp;'static str; N]` and `[Value; N]` are legal there, and `N` is inferred at every call site. Verified compiling. Cost: no `dyn ToonProjection`.

Container-level impl (verified output below):

```rust
impl ToonProjection&lt;8&gt; for SymbolsResult {
    const TYPE_NAME: &amp;'static str = "symbols";
    const FIELDS: [&amp;'static str; 8] =
        ["file","name","kind","line","signature","owner","traitImpl","visibility"];
    fn toon_rows(&amp;self) -&gt; Vec&lt;[Value; 8]&gt; {
        self.files.iter().flat_map(|(f, es)| es.iter().map(move |e| [
            cell(f.as_str()), cell(&amp;e.name), cell(e.kind.as_str()), Value::from(e.line),
            opt_cell(e.signature.as_deref()), opt_cell(e.owner.as_deref()),
            opt_cell(e.trait_impl.as_deref()), opt_cell(e.visibility.map(|v| v.as_str())),
        ])).collect()
    }
    fn toon_total_count(&amp;self) -&gt; Option&lt;usize&gt; { Some(self.total_symbol_count) }
    fn toon_hints(&amp;self) -&gt; Vec&lt;Hint&gt; { /* Diagnostics + truncation_marker → Hints */ }
}
```

Verified output:
```
symbols[2]{file,name,kind,line,signature,owner,traitImpl,visibility}:
  src/auth.ts,login,function,12,"function login(u: string, p: string): Promise&lt;Token&gt;",,,
  src/lib.rs,new,method,40,"fn new(a: A, b: B) -&gt; Self",Cache,Default,public
totalCount: 137
help[2]:
  skipped: vendor/x.py: unsupported language
  [symbol cap 500 reached; pass fewer files or increase limit]
```

### The error adapter

```rust
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct ErrorContext&lt;'a&gt; { pub command: &amp;'a str }   // RecoveryHint.tool needs this

pub trait ToDomainError {
    /// Stable monokl discriminator, finer-grained than michi's 9 codes.
    /// Emitted as the message prefix so it survives every michi render path.
    fn monokl_code(&amp;self) -&gt; &amp;'static str;
    fn error_code(&amp;self) -&gt; ErrorCode;
    fn to_domain_error(&amp;self, ctx: ErrorContext&lt;'_&gt;) -&gt; DomainError;
}

/// thiserror's Display omits `#[source]`, and DomainError has no `source()`.
/// Flatten the chain, splitting on newlines: michi writes `message` and hints
/// RAW into line-oriented blocks (error.rs:177, hints.rs:58).
fn chain_lines(e: &amp;dyn std::error::Error) -&gt; Vec&lt;String&gt; { /* … */ }

impl ToDomainError for MonoklError {
    fn to_domain_error(&amp;self, ctx: ErrorContext&lt;'_&gt;) -&gt; DomainError {
        let mut lines = chain_lines(self);
        let head = if lines.is_empty() { String::new() } else { lines.remove(0) };
        let mut d = DomainError::new(self.error_code(),
                        format!("{}: {head}", self.monokl_code()))
            .retryable(false);                    // nothing monokl does is transient
        for l in lines { d = d.hint(l); }         // chain tail → help[] entries
        // per-variant enrichment, e.g.:
        if let Self::TooManyTerms { count, limit } = self {
            d = d.hint(format!("you supplied {count} terms; the limit is {limit}"))
                 .recovery(RecoveryHint::new(ctx.command)
                     .param("maxTerms", KvValue::Int(*limit as i64))
                     .reason("re-run with a shorter query"));
        }
        d
    }
}
// Deliberately NO `impl From&lt;MonoklError&gt; for DomainError` — it passes the
// orphan rule (verified), but would make the conversion reachable via `?`
// anywhere in the crate, defeating boundary containment.
```

Verified: `Io` + `PermissionDenied` → `error: forbidden` / `message: io: io failure` / `help[1]: os error 13` — the root cause reaches the agent. Multi-line git stderr → first line in `message`, remainder as a help entry; **both blocks stay well-formed**.

### Impact on gated specs

| Track | Change required |
|---|---|
| SPEC-001 `MonoklError` | **None.** The impl is external to the enum. |
| SPEC-003/005/006/007/008/009 | **None.** Local trait, local types — impls live in `output/agent.rs`. |
| SPEC-004 Cargo.toml | **Amendment required**: optional `michi` dep, `cli` feature edit, `rust-version` 1.85→1.96, `license` MIT→AGPL-3.0-or-later. |
| SPEC-004 lib.rs | **None.** `output` becomes a directory module. |

**One gated spec must be re-opened, and it's the cheapest one** — a manifest, not a type contract. Everything else layers on cleanly. That is the strongest practical argument for this pattern.

---

## 5. What monokl must build that michi doesn't provide

1. **The entire token budget layer.** o200k_base counting, list-level capping, three interacting caps, 2 MB ceiling, omitted-count marker. michi contributes nothing.
2. **The projection layer itself** — `ToonProjection` impls, `BTreeMap` flattening, the locator-vs-payload split for `search`.
3. **Newline normalization** for cells, error messages, and hints. michi actively destroys newlines on three paths and injects them un-escaped on two others.
4. **Error-chain flattening.** `DomainError` has no `source()`.
5. **A monokl error-code vocabulary.** 9 michi codes vs. 14 variants; five collapse.
6. **All hint and recovery *content*.** michi provides `Hint`/`RecoveryHint` as carriers and zero text. Per-variant hint generation is entirely monokl's.
7. **Pre-validation before every `AgentResponse` render** — because `toon_body()` swallows `validate()` errors into the body with exit code 0.
8. **A test-only TOON parser**, if a TOON surface ships — needed for round-trip property tests, not at runtime (monokl reads code, not TOON). michi has one to copy at `docs/projects/01-mvp.md:784`.
9. **The LSP/analyzer circuit breaker.** michi-resilience has no executor, no breaker, no async; `parse_retry_after`/`is_retryable_status` are HTTP concepts irrelevant to a local subprocess. Realistic reuse: **`next_retry_delay()`, one function.** States, counters, and the executor are monokl's.
10. **`--format toon` / `OutputProfile::Agent` plumbing**, including its interaction with `projection.rs`.

---

## 6. Sequencing

**Start with brainstorming, not canon.** ACTIVE.md's Pipeline Protocol constraint and CLAUDE.md both require `superpowers:brainstorming` for design decisions not covered by `docs/spec/05-research-and-decisions.md`. michi/TOON appears **nowhere** in the spec corpus — its only mentions are CLAUDE.md:7 (a forward-looking note) and an unrelated crate allowlist. Notably, ACTIVE.md records a canon-exit-gate failure on a **fabricated "TOON" citation** during SPEC-003, precisely because the term has no grounding in the corpus. So the new track's `linked_research` must cite **michi's real source files with line numbers**, not monokl documents.

Recommended order:

**P0 — infra, before any spec.** Settle license, MSRV, and how michi is consumed. Unblocking `orin-cargo` + `io-errors` is a prerequisite for *any* empirical verification from here on (`cargo metadata` fails today) — it already blocks every track after SPEC-004, so this isn't new debt introduced by michi.

**P1 — amend SPEC-004** with the manifest changes. Small, mechanical, but it re-opens a gated spec, so do it deliberately.

**P2 — brainstorming pass** on the three open design questions in §7.

**P3 — draft SPEC-010** (next free ID; 002 is reserved), *"michi output/error boundary."* Scope: `output/agent.rs` — `ToonProjection`, `cell`/`opt_cell`, `ToDomainError`, the 14-variant code map, chain flattening, line-discipline normalization, pre-validation. Its natural error cases are unusually rich and suit corner-case-first drafting: arity, newline corruption, `validate()` swallowing, retry-flag correctness, code-map exhaustiveness, `Null`-vs-empty-string ambiguity.

**Position: immediately before Track 21 (CLI / pipeline / main.rs, §20-22).** Track 21 consumes the boundary's public surface for `--format` dispatch, so the boundary must be gated first. All its inputs (SPEC-001, 003, 005, 006, 007, 008) are already gated.

**Keep Track 11 (Budget, §10) independent.** Different problem, no michi dependency, no ordering constraint either way.

**Bonus:** SPEC-001 explicitly non-goals the `render_error`-vs-miette conflict as "a known, separately-tracked gap." SPEC-010 is its natural home — `render_error` becomes `DomainError::render_json()`, which is well-tested and correctly escapes via `json_escape_str`, fixing both doc-07 finding #19 and the latent raw-`{err}`-into-JSON injection bug in one move.

---

## 7. Open questions I could not resolve from evidence

1. **`--only`/`--fields` vs. `const FIELDS: [_; N]`.** Projection is runtime-dynamic; the const-generic trait is static. Either declare `--format toon` incompatible with `--only` (clap conflict — simple, honest, recommended for v1), or add a dynamic escape hatch that re-introduces arity risk. If you go dynamic, call `ToonOptions::validate()` yourself first — michi's swallow-into-body behavior is unacceptable for monokl.

2. **Does `search --format toon` omit `code` entirely, or emit it `\n`-escaped?** Locators-plus-`extract` is cleaner and cheaper; `\n`-escaped is more self-contained but produces one enormous line. Possibly `--detail` decides. This is genuinely a product call, not a technical one.

3. **Should michi-truncate become an *unconditional* dep for defensive field caps in `budget.rs`?** It's ~90 lines with zero dependencies, and the AGPL obligation attaches either way. But it breaks "the library core is michi-free." My recommendation is to keep michi fully `cli`-gated for v1 and revisit as a separate one-line decision — but I don't think the evidence makes either answer clearly wrong.</result>

## All six claimed fixes are genuine

I re-derived every michi claim from source rather than trusting the fix narrative. The pin correction is real and complete:

- `cb355b8c…` is a real commit; `git rev-list --count v0.1.0..cb355b8` = **exactly 68**; the rev's root `Cargo.toml:53` has `miette = ["michi-core/miette"]`; `git grep -E 'default_class|ToonError|fn validate' v0.1.0` returns **zero** matches.
- AC-003's rewritten consequence is exactly right — the guard is verbatim `if fields.is_empty() &amp;&amp; !obj.is_empty() { … }`, `rows.push` does emit a zero-length row, and `opts.validate()?` runs unconditionally before `render_toon()`.
- AC-010's format justification now matches reality: `DomainError::render()` writes a raw `message:` line, and `append_hints()` writes `help[N]:\n` + `  {hint}\n` **with no sanitization**.
- AC-017 is gone; grounding confirmed (`crates/monokl/src/` holds only `lib.rs`; clap appears in no `.rs` file).

AC-001, AC-011, AC-013, AC-014, AC-009, AC-016 also re-verified as stated. I compiled AC-004's trait declaration on stable rustc — the arity guarantee is real.

## It fails on four hard blockers that are new ground, not pin fallout

The previous gate's blanket clearance of AC-002/004/005/006/007/008/009/011/013/014/016 does not survive contact with the upstream type definitions:

1. **`ErrorContext` is undefined** (AC-008, api_surface, consumed by AC-012's `RecoveryHint::new(ctx.command)`). Zero matches in michi at the rev; in monokl it appears *only inside SPEC-010 itself*.
2. **`SymbolKind::as_str()` / `Visibility::as_str()` do not exist** (AC-005, AC-006). The entire corpus has one `fn as_str`, at `01-core-architecture.md:1393`, on an unrelated newtype. No `impl SymbolKind` or `impl Visibility` anywhere.
3. **Five of AC-006's seven column expressions don't compile.** `RankedBlock.block` is a plain named field — `#[serde(flatten)]` is serialization-only and there is no `Deref` (zero hits corpus-wide), so `block.file` etc. need `block.block.file`. AC-006 also writes bare `rank`/`final_score`, implying an unstated destructuring.
4. **AC-010's chain walk has two readings.** "a walk *over self* … as a `&amp;dyn Error`" vs. "*starting from* `self.source()`". Only Io/Walk/RegexBuild/Json carry a source, and the readings diverge materially there — reading B silently drops `Walk`'s `path` and `Json`'s locked Display text, contradicting the purpose field's "information-preserving" claim.

Five lower-severity blockers round it out: AC-015 says `DomainError::default_class()` where the method lives on `impl ErrorCode` (AC-011 gets it right — the spec contradicts itself); AC-004 miscites AC-001 for the `debug_assert!` finding (true fact, wrong source, asserted by no criterion); AC-003 miscites SPEC-003 AC-006 (which is about `deny_unknown_fields`); AC-010's "multi-line subprocess stderr" gloss on `Git.message` is unsourced; and AC-015's "HEAD is 1 commit past" is now false (it's 2).

No TBDs. Error cases properly covered (6 criteria with `is_error_case: true`). Scope fits one planning cycle. Condition 1 — every criterion a testable proposition requiring no implementer judgment — is what fails.

`retry_count: 2`, below the escalation threshold. **If a third round leaves blockers 1–3 open, escalate**: all three are cases where SPEC-010 depends on API surface its upstream specs never locked, which may mean SPEC-003/SPEC-006 need amendment rather than this spec.</result>

**Verdict: FAIL** (retry_count now 3 — at the escalation threshold).

The round-2 fixes are all genuine; I re-derived each one and every michi behavioral claim from primary source. Seven new defects blocked the pass:

**The one that matters most (B-2)** is a specification hole, not a citation error. Verified at the pinned rev in `crates/michi-core/src/response.rs`: `AgentResponse` has no API that accepts a `ToonOptions` — `toon_body()` builds its own internally from the builder's fields. So AC-014's instruction to validate "before ever constructing an AgentResponse from the ToonOptions" is unimplementable as written. Worse, nothing in `scope`, the 16 criteria, or the 9 `api_surface` entries defines the function that actually renders a `ToonProjection&lt;N&gt;`. `toon_total_count()` and `toon_hints()` are defined in AC-004 and AC-006's extract hint is required — but no specified code path consumes either.

**B-1** is the recurrence of the round-1/round-2 defect class. `non_goals[8]` claims no clap Args, Format enum, or cli.rs/main.rs exist "anywhere in this project's spec corpus or codebase." The codebase half is true. The corpus half is false: `docs/spec/01-core-architecture.md:2334` is "Section 21: cli.rs and output.rs verbatim" with a full `#[derive(Parser)] pub struct Cli`, `:2443` is "Section 22: main.rs verbatim", and `docs/spec/03-multi-language-platform.md:2177` declares

```rust
pub enum OutputFormat { Auto, Json, Minimal, Text, Mermaid, Schema }
```

wired as a global `--format` ValueEnum. That enum has **no `Toon` variant** — so `--format toon`, which this spec's scope, non_goals, and AC-006 all hang on, exists in no source document, and no criterion says to add it. This same false finding is what justified deleting AC-017 in round 2.

**B-3** is another false-fact-in-a-criterion: AC-010 says Json's Display "carries monokl-specific framing around the wrapped `serde_json::Error`'s message." `01-core-architecture.md:222-223` reads `#[error("json serialization failed")] Json(#[from] serde_json::Error),` — a fixed literal with no interpolation. Same class as round 2's "subprocess stderr" defect, same criterion.

**B-4/B-5/B-6/B-7** are miscitations: `T-023` is in `PLAN-004.json`, not SPEC-004 (which has zero task IDs), and describes a different technique; michi *is* mentioned in `docs/spec/` (`02-inspection-and-analysis.md:1713`); `03-multi-language-platform.md:1540-1551` doesn't contain the `format!` it's cited for (it's at 1554/1557/1562); `01-core-architecture.md:3006-3011` clips `io_source`'s `#[must_use]` at 3005.

Per my rule, retry_count 3 does not *exceed* 3, so this is a fail rather than an escalate. But I'd flag for the caller: three rounds have each surfaced fresh factual defects that survived the prior round because nobody re-checked them. The fix should be a single full re-derivation of all ~40 empirical assertions, not seven more targeted patches. A fourth failing round should go to a human.

## Residual observations (not blockers)

You asked me to flag anything suspect on a fresh whole-document read. Seven items, none individually fail-worthy, all of the same drift class:

- **AC-004** cites `crates/michi-toon/src/render.rs:140-144` for the arity `debug_assert!`. The construct spans 140-147 (`#[cfg(debug_assertions)]` at 140, `for` at 141, macro 142-146, closing brace 147). `:140-144` clips the macro mid-message — the same clipping defect round 3 corrected in AC-009. Should be `:140-147`.
- **AC-015** says the post-pin commits are "confined to test-only changes in the unrelated michi-node package." The 9 commits touch `packages/michi-node/__test__/index.test.mjs`, `docs/spec/04-mcp-and-napi.md`, and `src/napi/num.rs`. The last is in the `michi` root crate itself — the crate monokl depends on. The change is inside `#[cfg(test)] mod tests`, so "zero effect on the crates this track depends on" survives, but "confined to the michi-node package" is false.
- **AC-013** lists `visibility` among "monokl `Option&lt;String&gt;` field[s]" passed to `opt_cell`. It is `Option&lt;Visibility&gt;` (SPEC-003 AC-004). The `opt_cell` api_surface entry correctly omits it — so the two disagree with each other.
- **AC-005** writes bare "signature (opt_cell)", "owner (opt_cell)", etc., while AC-006 explicitly spells out `opt_cell(block.block.symbol_signature.as_deref())`. SymbolEntry's fields are `Option&lt;String&gt;` and `opt_cell` takes `Option&lt;&amp;str&gt;`, so the `.as_deref()` is load-bearing and only stated in one of the two parallel criteria.
- **AC-004B never sets `max_cell_len`.** Both the standalone `ToonOptions` (default 200) and `AgentResponse` (`DEFAULT_TRUNCATE_CELLS = 200`) therefore silently inline-truncate every string cell past 200 bytes via `michi_truncate::truncate_inline(s, 200, "full=true")`. `signature` and `symbolSignature` routinely exceed that. No criterion says whether this truncation is intended, and AC-006's whole locator-only rationale is about cell size — this looks like an unstated decision rather than a considered one.
- **AC-006's hint** names "file/lineStart/lineEnd parameters (ExtractRequest, SPEC-008 AC-007)". ExtractRequest's Rust fields are `file`/`line_start`/`line_end`; the camelCase forms are derived JSON keys via `rename_all`. Defensible for agent-facing hint text, but the citation implies the Rust names.
- **AC-015's `default-features = false`** is a no-op — michi's root package declares `default = []`. Harmless, but the criterion presents it as a deliberate, load-bearing choice.

## Bottom line

Round 4 does not pass, and per protocol it escalates rather than cycling. The re-derivation pass genuinely worked on the round-3 findings — AC-004B's michi mechanics are substantially correct, and I confirmed the great majority of its API claims verbatim. But the same defect class survived again in three distinct forms: a false empirical claim inside the newest and highest-risk criterion (B1), a fix asserted in `revision_note` that was never applied to the criterion it names (B2), and a fresh wrong-range citation in newly written text (B4). B2 is the one I would put in front of a human first — the document now misreports its own correction history, which means the `revision_note` can no longer be used as evidence that anything was fixed.
