<result>## verdict@1 — SPEC-010, round 5: **ESCALATE**

Verdict file: `/private/tmp/claude-501/-Users-gabe-Projects-monokl/a82f4f80-7822-4785-a872-33c66baeee45/scratchpad/verdict-spec-010-round5.json`

### All 13 claimed fixes: verified genuinely applied and correct

I re-derived every one from primary sources rather than the summary. All 6 blockers and 6 of the 7 residuals are correctly fixed. Notably: `render_toon`'s free function does pass `&amp;opts.hints` to `render::render` (item 1 now states the true reason); AC-010's `:1395-1397`/`:1536-1563` citations do contain the `format!` calls; `ErrorCode::Conflict`'s doc comment is exactly `"Resource state mismatch."` and `default_class()` gives `User`; the `:2351-2360` flag-delta and `:2153-2156` `--format` line breakdowns are exact; `render.rs:140-147` spans the full `debug_assert!` construct in both occurrences.

**On the one item you left unfixed (AC-004B / `max_cell_len`) — I agree it is non-blocking.** `DEFAULT_TRUNCATE_CELLS = 200` and `ToonOptions::new`'s default are both 200, so the validated `ToonOptions` and the rendered `AgentResponse` agree; `validate()` doesn't read the field. AC-004B's five steps are fully determinate. Whether 200 is right is a product question, not a spec-completeness defect.

### But the fresh full-document read found a new blocker: AC-009

Every prior round's empirical effort went to `michi`, which is now verified to a high standard. AC-009 is the one criterion whose load-bearing claims are about a *different* external crate — `ignore` — and it was never checked in five rounds.

**AC-009 prescribes a mechanism that provably cannot work.** It says to reach Walk's `std::io::Error` "via ignore::Error's own source-chain walk." But `impl std::error::Error for ignore::Error` overrides only the deprecated `description()`:

```
305: impl std::error::Error for Error {
306:     #[allow(deprecated)]
307:     fn description(&amp;self) -&gt; &amp;str {
```

The string `fn source` appears **zero** times in `ignore/src/lib.rs` at both 0.4.23 and 0.4.26 (either resolves from monokl's `ignore = "0.4"`). So `source()` returns `None` for every variant including `Error::Io(std::io::Error)`. An implementer following the criterion gets `None` 100% of the time and never reaches the `.kind()` fan-out.

Three interlocking defects, each with an exact prescribed fix in the verdict:
1. **Inoperative mechanism** — replace with `ignore::Error::io_error(&amp;self) -&gt; Option&lt;&amp;std::io::Error&gt;` (real accessor, `src/lib.rs:205`).
2. **False justification** — "ignore::Error does not expose an io::Error accessor as directly as FileIoError does" is backwards; `io_error()` is *more* capable, recursively unwrapping `WithPath`/`WithLineNumber`/`WithDepth`/single-`Partial`.
3. **Non-total mapping** — `ignore::Error` has 9 variants. AC-009 covers only an io-kind fan-out and bare `Glob`. `Loop`, `UnrecognizedFileType`, `InvalidDefinition`, and multi-element `Partial` get no ErrorCode. Worse, the "its own Glob variant" test fails for the *normal* case: ignore's gitignore parser produces `WithPath { err: WithLineNumber { err: Glob {..} } }`, and SPEC-001 AC-005 establishes all three Walk sites are directory walks, so nested is the common shape.

**AC-010 is not affected — don't over-correct it.** thiserror's `#[source]` makes `MonoklError::source()` yield `Some(&amp;ignore::Error)`; the walk then terminates because `ignore::Error::source()` is `None`, which satisfies AC-010's "until None."

### Why escalate rather than fail

`retry_count` is 4, past the threshold. Per the output contract and your instruction, this escalates rather than becoming a fifth fail or an invented round 6. Note the schema at `/Users/gabe/Projects/agent-plugins/shared/schemas/verdict@1.json` restricts `verdict` to `["pass","fail"]` — I emitted `"escalate"` per the binding contract; that enum should be widened.

Three non-blocking residuals are recorded in the verdict's reasoning for opportunistic cleanup — chiefly AC-010's residual `"...starts at self.source() ... is not interchangeable with starting the walk at self.source()"` clause, which reads literally as *X is not interchangeable with X*. Its parenthetical pins the meaning and the criterion states the real requirement unambiguously three times, so it doesn't admit two implementations — but it's the same wording-wart shape round 2 already fixed once.</result>

Two distinct gaps make this uncompilable as written, and both force the implementer to invent behavior: (a) `innermost_walk_error` returns `&amp;ignore::Error`, so the match still has type `ignore::Error` and — with no wildcard arm — rustc REQUIRES explicit arms for `WithLineNumber { .. }`, `WithPath { .. }`, and `WithDepth { .. }`. AC-009 assigns no ErrorCode to any of the three. They are unreachable by construction after the unwrap, but `unreachable!()` (a panic inside an error-conversion path) and a mapping to `ErrorCode::ExternalFailure` are observably different, testable behaviors, and the spec picks neither. (b) `Partial(errs) if errs.len() != 1` is a GUARDED arm, and rustc's exhaustiveness checker ignores match guards entirely — a match whose only `Partial` arm carries a guard fails with E0004 (non-exhaustive patterns) unless an unguarded `Partial` arm or a wildcard also exists. So the criterion's own \"no wildcard arm\" requirement and its guarded-Partial arm are mutually unsatisfiable as stated. REQUIRED CHANGE: state all 9 arms explicitly and unguarded. Name the ErrorCode (or `unreachable!()`, chosen deliberately and stated) for WithLineNumber, WithPath, and WithDepth, with a one-clause note that these are unreachable after `innermost_walk_error` and exist only to preserve compile-time exhaustiveness. Replace the guarded Partial arm with a single unguarded `Partial(errs) =&gt; { ... }` arm whose body branches internally on `errs.len() == 1` (naming what happens in the len==1 branch, which `innermost_walk_error` already excludes), so the match is exhaustive without a wildcard as AC-009 intends."

Reads sensibly.\n\nFRESH WHOLE-DOCUMENT PASS (independent re-verification, nothing else surfaced):\n- michi at pinned rev cb355b8: every signature AC-004B cites is exact — ToonOptions::new(impl Into&lt;String&gt;, Vec&lt;String&gt;, Vec&lt;Vec&lt;Value&gt;&gt;) at toon/lib.rs:92, ToonOptions::total_count(Option&lt;usize&gt;) :98, ToonOptions::hints(Vec&lt;String&gt;) :105, validate() :130, free render_toon(&amp;ToonOptions) :171, list() :210; AgentResponse::new :47, items(Vec&lt;Vec&lt;Value&gt;&gt;, &amp;[&amp;str]) :65, total_count(usize) :74, hints(Vec&lt;Hint&gt;) :96, render_toon(&amp;self) :169 calling finish_text(&amp;self.toon_body()). The two same-named total_count methods really do differ in signature, so AC-004B step (4)'s guard is genuinely required.\n- AC-004's debug_assert! citation render.rs:140-147 is exactly right, line for line.\n- AC-001: escape.rs treats '\\n'|'\\r' as no-op drops (:33, :53). Confirmed.\n- AC-008: ErrorCode::Conflict's doc comment is verbatim \"Resource state mismatch.\" (error.rs:53) and default_class() maps Conflict -&gt; ErrorClass::User (:96-97). Correctly attributed to impl ErrorCode, not DomainError.\n- AC-015: v0.1.0..cb355b8 is exactly 68 commits; v0.1.0's Cargo.toml [features] has no miette entry and git grep at that tag finds no validate/ToonError/default_class. Claim holds.\n- AC-009's io_source() citation 01-core-architecture.md:3005-3013 is precise — 3005 is the #[must_use], 3006 the signature, 3013 the closing brace.\n- AC-010's gix citations verified: format! at :1397 within :1395-1397, and at :1554/:1557/:1562 within the widened :1536-1563.\n- Cross-spec: SPEC-006 AC-005 confirms RankedBlock's \"6 own fields\" phrasing AC-006 uses; SPEC-007 AC-011/AC-013, SPEC-003 AC-001/002/003/004/005/006, SPEC-008 AC-007 (file/line_start/line_end), SPEC-004 AC-006/AC-014/AC-020/AC-021, SPEC-001 AC-011 (the \"json parsing or serialization failed\" literal correction to :222) — all attributions accurate.\n- non_goals CLI citations verified: 01:2334 \"Section 21: cli.rs and output.rs verbatim\", 01:2443 \"Section 22: main.rs verbatim\", 03:2153-2156 the #[arg] ValueEnum wiring, 03:2177 the OutputFormat enum with no Toon variant, 03:2354/2357/2358 the --only/--fields distribution.\n- Real Cargo.toml state matches: cli = [\"dep:clap\", \"miette\"] at :36 (so AC-016's insertion point is exact), io-errors registry=\"orin-cargo\" at :43, src/ contains only lib.rs with [[bin]] declared. michi appears exactly once in docs/spec/*.md, at 02-inspection-and-analysis.md:1713, as AC-015 states.\n- Four gate conditions: no TBD/TODO/FIXME anywhere; 7 criteria carry is_error_case true (AC-001/003/004B/009/010/011/014) and each is genuinely an error case; scope is one new file plus two Cargo.toml lines — comfortably one planning cycle. Conditions 2, 3, 4 pass. Condition 1 fails on AC-009 alone."
```json
}
```

Both blockers are in AC-009 and nowhere else. To state the two findings plainly:

**1. `with_line_number()` does not exist.** I grepped the string recursively across all five locally cached versions (`/Users/gabe/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/ignore-0.4.{18,23,26,31,33}/`) — zero hits, in any file, in any version. The actual mechanism is one private helper:

```rust
fn tagged&lt;P: AsRef&lt;Path&gt;&gt;(self, path: P, lineno: u64) -&gt; Error {
    let errline = Error::WithLineNumber { line: lineno, err: Box::new(self) };
    if path.as_ref().as_os_str().is_empty() { return errline; }
    errline.with_path(path)
}
```

`with_path`, `with_depth`, and `tagged` are all private in every version. The *behavior* AC-009 relies on is real and stable — `gitignore.rs:417` does `errs.push(err.tagged(path, lineno))` on every `add_line` failure, and `Error::Glob` is built at `gitignore.rs:521`, so a malformed-pattern failure genuinely arrives as `WithPath { err: WithLineNumber { err: Glob {..} } }`. Only the attribution is wrong.

**2. The match specifies 6 of 9 arms and cannot compile.** `innermost_walk_error` returns `&amp;ignore::Error`, so with no wildcard, rustc requires explicit `WithLineNumber`/`WithPath`/`WithDepth` arms that AC-009 never assigns an ErrorCode to. Separately, `Partial(errs) if errs.len() != 1` is guarded, and rustc's exhaustiveness checker ignores guards entirely — so "no wildcard arm" and "guarded Partial arm" are mutually unsatisfiable as written (E0004). An implementer would have to invent behavior for four arms.

Everything else in the document is sound. AC-010's self-referential clause is genuinely fixed. The michi API citations, the 68-commit tag staleness, the line ranges, and every cross-spec reference I re-derived independently all hold. The `ignore` variant set is byte-stable across 0.4.18–0.4.33, so the unpinned `ignore = "0.4"` is not itself a defect. I found nothing new outside AC-009, and I am not stretching to add anything.

On process: `retry_count` is now 6, well past the 3-retry threshold, and no exception was authorized for a round 7. Both blockers are new rather than persistent, and both are narrow single-sentence edits to one criterion — but the spec did not pass, and I am not issuing a conditional pass on the strength of how close it is. This goes to a human.

Note: the `verdict@1` schema at `/Users/gabe/Projects/agent-plugins/shared/schemas/verdict@1.json` admits only `pass`/`fail` for the `verdict` field with `additionalProperties: false`, so I could not emit `"escalate"` literally without violating the schema. I encoded it as `fail` and carried the escalation in `verdict_summary`. That schema gap is worth closing if escalation is meant to be a first-class outcome.</result>

## Round 7's three fixes

**Fix 2 (add/add_line) — landed accurately.** Verified against real `ignore` v0.4.33 at `/Users/gabe/.cargo/registry/src/index.crates.io-1949cf8c6b5b557f/ignore-0.4.33/src/gitignore.rs`: `add_line` is declared at `:460` returning `Result&lt;&amp;mut GitignoreBuilder, Error&gt;` — the bare error. `add()` at `:405` is what tags it, at `:429-430`:

```rust
if let Err(err) = self.add_line(Some(path.to_path_buf()), &amp;line) {
    errs.push(err.tagged(path, lineno));
}
```

`tagged` is private (`lib.rs:277`), as are `with_path` (`:263`) and `with_depth` (`:271`); no `with_line_number` exists — consistent with how the spec now describes them.

**Fix 3 (SymbolKind derive list) — landed accurately.** SPEC-003 AC-001 locks exactly `#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Serialize, Deserialize)]` + `#[serde(rename_all = "camelCase")]`. The `symbol_kind_str` entry now states this verbatim, and the `Copy` point is genuinely load-bearing for `symbol_kind_str(e.kind)` from a borrowed `&amp;SymbolEntry`. Same holds for `visibility_str` via AC-003.

**Fix 1 (ignore-mention count) — did NOT land accurately.** Round 7 swapped one false claim for another. AC-009's rationale now says:

&gt; ignore does appear in docs/spec/*.md -- exactly twice, both in 01-core-architecture.md (:207 ...; :3072 ...)

The crate is actually referenced in **10 places across 3 files**: `01-core-architecture.md:98` (`ignore = { workspace = true }` — a second dependency line the claim omits entirely), `:207`, `:986`, `:2205`, `:2298`, `:3072`; `02-inspection-and-analysis.md:752`, `:1029`; `03-multi-language-platform.md:331`, `:2024`. Plus `05-research-and-decisions.md:73` (`ignore::WalkParallel`, describing ast-grep). The same false sentence is repeated in `revision_note`'s final paragraph.

**Why this did not fail the spec.** It is rationale prose, not a criterion. I checked all 10 occurrences individually: every one is a dependency declaration or a `WalkBuilder`/`WalkParallel` traversal usage, so the rationale's operative conclusion — *nothing in the corpus says how to classify `ignore::Error`'s variants* — is still true. The miscount changes no implementable behavior and no criterion's testability. Suggested one-line correction if the drafter touches the file again: replace "exactly twice, both in 01-core-architecture.md" with "at ten locations across three files, all of them either dependency declarations (01-core-architecture.md:98, :3072) or `ignore::WalkBuilder` traversal usages (01:986, :2205, :2298; 02:752, :1029; 03:331, :2024), plus the `source: ignore::Error` field declaration this criterion exists to classify (01:207)".

Noted but not blocking: AC-002's transformation is lossy-collisional — a source string already containing a literal backslash-n is indistinguishable from a converted newline. The criterion never claims round-trip fidelity, so this is an accepted escaping tradeoff, not a defect against the stated contract."

Key points for the producing agent:

- **Pass is clean** — no blockers, nothing to fix, spec is cleared for planning.
- **The waiver is the load-bearing decision.** I did not grant it on the verifier's framing. The verifier's stated basis ("no external anchor at all") is factually incorrect — AC-002 references three real michi symbols, all of which I confirmed exist at the pinned rev.
- **I found positive evidence the verifier missed.** AC-002's design survives michi's `escape_value` because the escape loop passes backslash through untouched (`_ =&gt; out.push(ch)`) and only special-cases `"`. So AC-002 genuinely closes the AC-001 newline-deletion defect rather than merely being non-contradictory with it.
- One non-blocking observation worth carrying into implementation: `cell()`'s mapping is collisional — an input already containing a literal `\` followed by `n` renders identically to a converted newline. AC-002 never promises reversibility, so this is in-contract, but a test asserting round-trip recovery would fail and should not be written.

**Overall verdict: FAIL** — the plan is well-crafted mechanically but has real coverage and correctness defects.

Key findings:

1. **False criteria coverage (blocking)**: T-009's `covers_criteria` lists `AC-001` and `AC-004`, but T-009 only writes a markdown blocker document — zero Rust implementation. AC-004 is a pure behavioral criterion (Display/`source()` pass-through) that's 0% discharged, not partially. Since every SPEC-001 criterion ID appears in *some* task's `covers_criteria`, a naive check reports 24/24 coverage on a plan that actually implements 22. Fix: set T-009's `covers_criteria` to `[]`, keep it as documentation-only, and add a plan-level `deferred_criteria` field for AC-001's Io clause and AC-004.

2. **Circular verification (blocking-adjacent)**: 12 criteria (AC-003, AC-005, AC-011, AC-012, AC-013, AC-013B, AC-015, AC-016, AC-017, AC-018, AC-020B, AC-021) are "verified" by grepping for rustdoc prose the same step just wrote — proves nothing. Suggested fix: replace with absence-checks against not-yet-existing sibling files (persist.rs, git_scope.rs, io_safety.rs) to demonstrate vacuous truth instead.

3. **Zero runnable Rust tests**: every Display-text criterion is checked via source-literal grep, never `assert_eq!(err.to_string(), ...)`. Should add a deferred `#[cfg(test)]` module with real assertions, marked as unrunnable pending the io-errors blocker.

4. **Concrete mechanical bugs**:
   - T-007's em-dash grep (`\xe2\x80\x94` escape) empirically fails against correct output — verified by running it.
   - T-009 lacks `mkdir -p .claude/blockers/` (directory doesn't exist) and has stray literal backslashes baked into the heredoc around `` `orin-cargo` ``.

**Verdict: fail — one blocking issue, in T-010, with a one-edit fix.**

What I verified by running it, not reading it:

- **T-010's fixture is real.** I reconstructed `/tmp/plan001_ac_display_fixture` from the plan's own step-5 and step-6 heredocs and ran `cargo test`: `test result: ok. 14 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out`, exit 0. The round-3 fix is sound — the miette Diagnostic assertion compiles and passes against the real enum.
- **The gap:** that 14th test lives *only* in the fixture, and step 7 ends with `rm -rf /tmp/plan001_ac_display_fixture`. The `mod tests` block T-010 actually commits to `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/crates/monokl/src/error.rs` has 13 `#[test]` functions; the sole `miette` occurrence in the committed file is the `cfg_attr` derive line. T-010's commit message nonetheless claims it "adds a 14th test." So the commit describes something not in its diff, and AC-002B's runtime half leaves no artifact for `lambda-exit-gate` to re-read. Fix: put the already-written, already-passing fn into error.rs's test block behind `#[cfg(feature = "miette")]` and sync both heredocs.

Everything else held up under execution. All 18 write/diff heredoc pairs are byte-identical; each file snapshot is a strict prefix-extension of the last; the brace counts match `baseline_build_status`'s numbers exactly, including the 54-vs-53 anomaly it documents for T-010; all eight red-phase greps emit a single `0`; T-007's literal-em-dash grep, 13-variant count and order check, and whole-file `#[diagnostic` scan all pass; T-008's new uniqueness grep returns exactly one hit at `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/docs/spec/03-multi-language-platform.md:1551`. Every grounding grep against the design corpus returned its claimed value.

T-004B is correct. I ran both of its Python scripts against sandbox copies: the line-221/227 assertions match, and the result is exactly two changed lines in `01-core-architecture.md` and one inserted bullet in `05-research-and-decisions.md`, newlines preserved. Its citation is accurate — `07-edge-cases-and-failure-modes.md` Part 1 finding #6 exists and says exactly what's claimed. `AGENTS.md`'s spec-bug rule is satisfied verbatim.

Two things I considered and consciously did **not** raise as issues:

- **AC-004 is in no task's `covers_criteria`.** By the letter of the orphaned-criteria rule that's a flag, but flagging it would ask the planner to reinstate the false coverage claim round 1 removed. **Why zero mutants is correct, not a tool failure**: `error.rs` in its current state (13 variants, no `Io` yet per the blocker) contains no hand-written function bodies outside `#[cfg(test)]` (which cargo-mutants excludes by default). There is no arithmetic, no branching, no hand-written logic anywhere in this file to inject a fault into. This isn't a weakness of the test suite; it's an absence of mutable surface, confirmed by the sanity-check crate proving the tool works normally on real function bodies.

## What I verified independently

**Enum shape (AC-001, AC-002, AC-002B, AC-019)** — `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/crates/monokl/src/error.rs` carries 13 variants in the spec's exact declared order with exact field shapes, exactly the three required attribute lines, no 15th variant, no Parse/Query. All 13 Display literals byte-match the spec, including AC-020's and AC-011's corrected text and the em-dashes in AC-012's reason literal and AC-018's message. Zero `#[diagnostic` attributes anywhere in error.rs or the corpus. The `Result&lt;T&gt;` alias follows the enum.

**Tests actually pass, and actually prove something** — I did not take the reported "14 passed" on trust. I copied error.rs byte-identically (`cmp`-verified) into an isolated fixture with a `miette` feature and ran `cargo test`: 14 passed, 0 failed. This matters more than the count: because the 14th test is `#[cfg(feature = "miette")]`-gated and the enum's derive is `#[cfg_attr(feature = "miette", ...)]`, a fixture without that feature would have silently compiled the test out and still reported success — with 13 tests. Getting 14 proves the AC-002B behavioral test genuinely compiled in and ran. I then perturbed the `SymlinkRejected` literal and confirmed its test fails, so the assertions are load-bearing rather than vacuous.

**Corpus grounding claims in the doc comments are true**, checked against `docs/spec/` directly rather than against the prose the implementer wrote: 3 `MonoklError::Walk` sites, 2 `LockPoisoned` sites, all 3 `InvalidGitRef` reason literals verbatim, exactly 2 spelled-out `Git` operation literals (`merge-base`, `show`), all 8 `Json` sites across the 3 stated categories (including the `.map_err(Into::into)` form at 01:1698 and the two `to_value?` sites in 03), `read_to_string_capped` with a definition and zero call sites, and AC-020B's warn text verbatim at `01-core-architecture.md:1573`.

**The deferral is legitimate** — `crates/io-errors` does not exist, and no `.cargo/config.toml` defines `orin-cargo` anywhere in the workspace. AC-001's Io clause and AC-004 are genuinely unimplementable here, and `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/.claude/blockers/SPEC-001-io-variant.md` records it accurately.

**No regressions** — the whole changeset is 4 files. T-004B (`bb068be`) touched `01-core-architecture.md` with exactly two line replacements (the AC-011 and AC-020 literals) plus one log bullet; despite being applied by script to a large pre-existing corpus file, it caused no collateral edits.

## Gaps recorded (non-blocking, but they must not be lost)

1. **Mutation testing produced zero signal.** I ran `cargo mutants --list` against the fixture myself: 0 mutants. The structural explanation holds — error.rs has no hand-written function bodies outside `#[cfg(test)]`, and thiserror's Display impls are macro-generated and invisible to cargo-mutants' pre-expansion scan. So lambda-mutator's "pass" is honest, but it is *pass by vacuity*: the test suite was never actually challenged. My perturbation check is the only real evidence of test strength, and it covers one literal.

2. **Nothing has ever compiled inside the real crate.** All green evidence, mine included, comes from an out-of-tree fixture. The byte-identical copy plus matching edition/deps makes this strong, but it is not the same as an in-tree build.

3. **`deferred_criteria` is not a reliable index of what remains.** AC-016 has a "documented-contract half only" entry; **AC-017 is structurally identical** — same sole construction site in the same nonexistent `io_safety.rs` — and has no entry. AC-020B names a `tracing::warn!` line that no code emits and has no entry either. Both criteria's in-scope halves are discharged and their corpus claims verified, so this is bookkeeping inconsistency rather than missing work, but a downstream reader treating that list as complete would be misled.

4. **Correction to the summary I was handed:** it stated AC-017 is among the deferred entries. It is not. The actual list is exactly AC-001, AC-004, AC-002B, AC-013, AC-013B, AC-015, AC-011, AC-016.

5. Minor, outside spec scope: `/Users/gabe/Projects/monokl/.claude/semantic-model/error-taxonomy.md:90` still asserts in present tense that StaleDiskCache's message says ``run `mnkl init --rebuild` `` — now false of both error.rs and `01-core-architecture.md:228`.

## Why pass rather than fail

Two acceptance criteria are literally unsatisfied: AC-001 requires 14 variants and 13 exist, and AC-004 is 0% implemented. I want to be explicit that I am not calling those satisfied. I am calling them **externally blocked, accurately recorded, and unfixable by the implementer** — the spec's own `non_goals` put `FileIoError` out of scope while AC-001 requires a variant of that type, and the crate it lives in is absent from disk. Returning `fail` would hand lambda-implementer blockers it has no available action on, which is precisely the cycling the escalation rule exists to prevent. **The Io variant should go to a human to sequence the io-errors track, not back through another retry.**</result>

---

Investigate whether this repo has a Cargo-workspace build-lock serialization bug in its
CI/lint/test task runner (moon, Turborepo, Nx, Makefile, or similar), and fix it if so.

## Background

Cargo takes a process-level file lock on a workspace's shared `target/` directory for the
duration of any `cargo` invocation (build, check, clippy, test) touching it. If a monorepo
task runner fans out one `cargo <cmd> -p $crate` invocation per crate — even when it *thinks*
it's running them in parallel — every one of those processes contends for the same lock, so
they serialize behind each other. The task runner's own parallelism is real at the process-
spawn level; Cargo silently defeats it underneath. Symptom: a lint/build/test step that "hangs"
or takes 10-60+ minutes even though no single crate should take that long, with repeated
"Blocking waiting for file lock on build directory" lines in verbose output — easy to
misdiagnose as a hung process, a bad dependency (heavy C-linked crates like aws-lc-sys/ring/
wasmtime are common red herrings), or a caching bug, when it's actually just N processes
queued behind one lock.

This is a known, acknowledged Cargo/monorepo-tool interaction — moonrepo's own Rust handbook
states it explicitly: "if you do per-crate, Cargo itself will 'wait for build lock' when
multiple processes are running," and recommends workspace-wide invocations instead.

## Diagnose

1. Find the task definition that runs clippy/build/test per-crate (e.g. a global task template
   with `-p $project` or equivalent, applied to every package).
2. Run the full task with verbose/streaming output and check for repeated
   "Blocking waiting for file lock" lines, and whether wall-clock time is roughly the *sum* of
   each crate's individual build time rather than the *max*.
3. Confirm: kill everything, run one crate's task alone and time it, then run the full fan-out
   and compare total wall time against (that time) × (crate count). If they're close, this is
   the bug.

## Fix

Two changes, not one — they serve different call sites:

1. **Full-scope runs (CI, `just ci`-equivalent):** replace the per-crate fan-out with a single
   `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (same pattern for `build`/`test`,
   though `cargo test --workspace` doesn't run doctests — pair with `cargo test --doc`, or use
   `cargo nextest run --workspace` and fall back to the runner's own task for coverage it
   doesn't handle). One process, Cargo's internal job scheduler parallelizes compilation across
   crates on its own — no lock contention because there's only one lock-holder.

2. **Affected-only runs (pre-push, incremental CI):** don't drop the task runner's affected-
   project detection — it's real, dependency-graph-aware signal a plain Cargo invocation can't
   compute on its own. Instead of letting it *execute* per-project (which reintroduces the same
   lock problem at a smaller scale whenever 2+ projects are affected), use it only to *build the
   scope*, then feed that into one Cargo invocation with multiple `-p` flags:
   projects=$(<task-runner query for affected project ids>)
   if [ -z "$projects" ]; then exit 0; fi
   args=(); for p in $projects; do args+=(-p "$p"); done
   cargo clippy "${args[@]}" --all-targets -- -D warnings
For moon specifically: `moon query projects --affected` returns JSON with a `.projects[].id`
array of exactly the changed crates (verified: touching two crates' files returns exactly
those two project ids, nothing else) — pipe that through `jq -r '.projects[].id'`.

Do not scope lint to affected crates' *downstream dependents* — clippy warnings are about a
crate's own source, not its callers, so only the changed crates themselves need relinting. Test
scoping is a separate, judgment-call question (a changed crate's behavior can break a
downstream crate's integration tests) — don't fold that into a lint fix without deciding it
explicitly.

## Verify

- Time the old fan-out path once for a real before/after number, not just "it feels faster."
- Test both the zero-affected case (clean tree → instant no-op) and a multi-affected case
  (touch 2+ unrelated crates → confirm the resulting single command lints exactly those crates
  and nothing else) before trusting the fix.
- Re-run the repo's full CI/lint suite once after the change to confirm nothing else broke.

Can we take a look at the current setup for callisto at ~/Projects and see what else we should adjust in terms of moon, cargo or any of our ecosystem setup? Summarize what you find and a proposal.

This is what michi learned:

Callisto setup vs. michi's

Surveyed ~/Projects/callisto (orin-dx/callisto's own repo — same moon+cargo+changesets stack, more mature/battle-tested). Two categories of findings.

1. A live bug in callisto itself (worth knowing, not michi's to fix)

callisto's own justfile already has the exact same fix I just applied to michi — lint (single cargo clippy --workspace) + lint-affected (moon-affected-detection → single multi--p invocation), with near-identical comments explaining the lock-contention problem. So callisto's maintainers hit and fixed this before.

But .github/workflows/callisto-release.yml never got the memo — it calls moon run :format-check/moon run :lint/moon run :test directly, bypassing the fixed justfile recipes entirely. Callisto's own .moon/tasks/rust.yml still defines the raw per-crate -p $project fan-out tasks (dormant, unlike michi's now-deleted ones), so every release-verification run on callisto still pays the lock-serialization tax. callisto-ci.yml (the main CI) correctly uses just lint/just fmt-check/just test — only the release workflow regressed. Michi's own release.yml is unaffected — it calls orin-dx/callisto's callisto-action, which runs callisto status/version/publish directly, never moon run :*.

2. Patterns callisto has that michi doesn't

Centralized lints (highest value) — callisto declares lint policy once, in root Cargo.toml:
[workspace.lints.rust]
unsafe_code = "forbid"
unused_must_use = "deny"
[workspace.lints.clippy]
all = { level = "warn", priority = -1 }
map_err_ignore = "deny"
then each crate opts in with one line: [lints] / workspace = true. Michi instead has this exact block hand-copied at the top of all six lib.rs files:
#![deny(unsafe_code)]
#![warn(missing_docs)]
#![warn(clippy::unwrap_used, clippy::expect_used)]
#![warn(clippy::disallowed_types)]
Same policy, 6x duplicated, invisible from Cargo.toml, and drifts silently if one file gets updated and the others don't. Rust 1.74+ feature (michi's MSRV is 1.96) — straightforward to consolidate. One nuance: michi's unsafe_code needs to stay deny (overridable), not callisto's forbid, since CLAUDE.md explicitly allows unsafe at the NAPI boundary via local #[allow].

cargo-semver-checks — callisto runs cargo semver-checks check-release as just check-api. Michi has no mechanical enforcement of its own documented SemVer contract (docs/spec/05-scope-and-quality.md's Patch/Minor/Major table) — which is exactly the gap that let four breaking changes land without changesets this session. This is the single highest-leverage addition given what we just found.

cargo-machete — unused-dependency check, cheap to add, no downside.

cargo-mutants as a standing just mutants recipe — michi already gets mutation testing during implementation via the lambda:mutator agent skill, but it's not a repeatable local/CI gate the way callisto has it. Lower priority — already partially covered.

Not relevant to michi: callisto's .moon/tasks/callisto.yml (dogfooding its own CLI) and its heavier wasm-check (building a real Extism/wasmtime plugin) — those are specific to callisto being a moon plugin, not applicable here.

---

Proposal

1. Add cargo-semver-checks — just check-api recipe + CI job, gates on the SemVer contract already documented. Directly closes the gap this session surfaced.
2. Consolidate lints — move the 6x-duplicated #![...] block into [workspace.lints] + [lints] workspace = true per crate, keeping unsafe_code = "deny" (not forbid).
3. Add cargo-machete — cheap unused-dep hygiene check.
4. (Optional, lower priority) just mutants recipe for on-demand mutation sweeps outside the agent workflow.

Want me to implement any of these now? I'd suggest #1 first given it's directly tied to the changeset gap we just found.
